\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

The shifts that appear are inessential, as they are removed later in the zig-zag. Note however that the coCartesian section on the right takes the value
(v_{i};0,S_{p_{i-1}}[1],0)
at
v_{i}
, which matches the value of a standard thimble at a vertex (up to a shift), see (
79
). Via such identifications, we indeed conclude in the end the required equivalence
\Delta_{i}^{\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}}\simeq\mu_{i}^{\sharp}(\Delta_{i-1}^{\mathfrak{w}})
.

The argument for the costandard weave thimbles in Part (2) is analogous. Finally, Part (3) follows from Part (2), as the corresponding equivalences
\mu_{i}^{\sharp}
and
\mu_{i}^{\flat}
between global sections are inverses of each other.
∎

8
Proofs of Theorem
B
.(2),
B
.(3) and
B
.(4)

The aim of this section is to prove Parts (2), (3) and (4) for Theorem
B
. These results were also stated as

Theorems

4.6
,
4.5
and
4.8
, respectively.

8.1
Categorical Lusztig cycles are a simple-minded collection

Let us now prove Theorem
B
.(2), or cf.
Theorem

4.6
, showing that the categorical Lusztig cycles form a simple-minded collection. Let
\mathfrak{w}
be Demazure weave
\mathfrak{w}
with
m
trivalent vertices,
(\Delta_{1}^{\mathfrak{w}},\ldots,\Delta_{m}^{\mathfrak{w}})
its standard weave thimbles and
(\nabla_{1}^{\mathfrak{w}},\ldots,\nabla_{m}^{\mathfrak{w}})
its costandard weave thimbles, as in
Definition

7.14
.

Definition 8.1
.

(1)

For
i\in[1,m]
, we define
\mathbb{S}_{i}^{\mathfrak{w}}\in R\Gamma({\bf G_{\mathfrak{w}}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w}})
to be the object

\mathbb{S}^{\mathfrak{w}}_{i}\coloneqq T_{\Delta^{\mathfrak{w}}_{1}}^{+,\geq 0}(\cdots(T_{\Delta^{\mathfrak{w}}_{i-1}}^{+,\geq 0}(\Delta^{\mathfrak{w}}_{i})))\,.

(2)

For
i\in[1,m]
, we define
\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{i}^{\mathfrak{w}}\in R\Gamma({\bf G_{\mathfrak{w}}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w}})
to be the object

\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}^{\mathfrak{w}}_{i}\coloneqq T_{\nabla^{\mathfrak{w}}_{1}}^{-,\leq 0}(\cdots(T_{\nabla^{\mathfrak{w}}_{i-1}}^{-,\leq 0}(\nabla^{\mathfrak{w}}_{i})))\,.

∎

It is proven in
Proposition

A.12
that
(\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\mathfrak{w}},\ldots,\mathbb{S}_{m}^{\mathfrak{w}})
is a simple-minded collection in
R\Gamma({\bf G_{\mathfrak{w}}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w}})
, and so is
(\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{1}^{\mathfrak{w}},\ldots,\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{m}^{\mathfrak{w}})
. In fact,
Lemma

A.22
implies the equivalences
\mathbb{S}^{\mathfrak{w}}_{i}\simeq\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}^{\mathfrak{w}}_{i}
for each
i\in[1,m]
. It will nevertheless be useful for the proofs to consider both types of objects based on their description in
Definition

8.1
. We also introduce a variant of
Definition

8.1
, using only partially iterated semi-twists, as follows. For
i\in[1,m]
and
j\in[i,m]
we define

\mathbb{S}_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{w}}\coloneqq T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i}^{\mathfrak{w}}}(\cdots(T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{j-1}^{\mathfrak{w}}}(\Delta_{j}^{\mathfrak{w}})))\,,

(94)

where
\mathbb{S}_{j,j}^{\mathfrak{w}}=\Delta_{j}^{\mathfrak{w}}
, and the case
\mathbb{S}_{1,j}^{\mathfrak{w}}=\mathbb{S}_{j}^{\mathfrak{w}}
recovers
Definition

8.1
.(1). Similarly, we define

\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{w}}\coloneqq T^{-,\leq 0}_{\nabla_{i}^{\mathfrak{w}}}(\cdots(T^{-,\leq 0}_{\nabla_{j-1}^{\mathfrak{w}}}(\nabla_{j}^{\mathfrak{w}})))\,.

(95)

To ease notation, we often drop the exponent
\mathfrak{w}
from these notations if
\mathfrak{w}
can be implicitly understood by context, e.g. we simply denote
\mathbb{S}_{j}\coloneqq\mathbb{S}_{j}^{\mathfrak{w}}
and
\mathbb{S}_{i,j}\coloneqq\mathbb{S}_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{w}}
in such a case.

Theorem 8.2
.

Let
\mathfrak{w}
be a Demazure weave with
m
trivalent vertices. Then:

1.

For each
i\in[1,m]
, there exists an equivalence
{\bm{L}}^{\mathfrak{w}}_{i}\simeq\mathbb{S}^{\mathfrak{w}}_{i}
and, similarly,
{\bm{L}}^{\mathfrak{w}}_{i}\simeq\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}^{\mathfrak{w}}_{i}\,
.

2.

The categorical Lusztig cycles
\{{\bm{L}}^{\mathfrak{w}}_{i}\}
form a simple-minded collection in
R\Gamma({\bf G_{\mathfrak{w}}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w}})
.

Proof of
Theorem

8.2
.

Let us fix
i\in[1,m]
and omit
\mathfrak{w}
during the proof, so as to ease notation.

Part (1).A
. Let us prove the equivalence
{\bm{L}}^{\mathfrak{w}}_{i}\simeq\mathbb{S}^{\mathfrak{w}}_{i}
in Part (1). First, for
j\in(i,m]
the values

{\bm{L}}_{i}(v_{j})\simeq\mathbb{S}_{i}(v_{j})\simeq 0,\quad j\in(i,m],

coincide at
v_{j}
because they vanish at
v_{j}
, cf.
Remark

4.2
. Similarly, for
j\in(i,m]
we have

{\bm{L}}_{i}(e_{j})\simeq\mathbb{S}_{i}(e_{j})\simeq 0,\quad j\in(i,m].

At the vertex
v_{i}
, the values of the sections
{\bm{L}}^{\mathfrak{w}}_{i}
and
\mathbb{S}^{\mathfrak{w}}_{i}
coincide by direct computation:

{\bm{L}}_{i}(v_{i})\simeq\mathbb{S}_{i}(v_{i})\simeq(v_{i};k[-1],0,0).

Indeed, this follows from using
Definition

4.1
for
{\bm{L}}_{i}
, and (
78
) with
l=0
for
\mathbb{S}_{i}
, or alternatively (
89
), as
\mathbb{S}_{i}(v_{i})\simeq\Delta_{i}(v_{i})
. For their values at the edge
e_{i}
we obtain

{\bm{L}}_{i}(e_{i})\simeq\mathbb{S}_{i}(e_{i})\simeq S_{p_{i}}

by using
Definition

4.1
for
{\bm{L}}_{i}
, and equivalence (
82
) for
\mathbb{S}_{i}
, again with
l=0
.

The goal now is thus showing that

{\bm{L}}_{i}(v_{j})\simeq\mathbb{S}_{i}(v_{j})\qquad\mbox{ and }\qquad{\bm{L}}_{i}(e_{j})\simeq\mathbb{S}_{i}(e_{j}),\quad\forall j\in[1,i).

(96)

Let us prove (
96
) iteratively, starting at
j=i-1
until we reach
j=1
. For that, fix
j\in(1,i)
, assume that (
96
) holds for any
k\in(j,i)
, and let
(\beta_{j+1},{\bf a}_{j+1})
be the weighted braid word associated to the Lusztig cycle
\gamma_{i}^{\mathfrak{w}}
at the horizontal slice directly above the vertex
v_{j}
. By
Definition

4.1
.(1), we have

{\bm{L}}_{i}(v_{j})=(v_{j};\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(S_{p_{j}},L_{\beta_{j+1},{\bf a}_{j+1}}),L_{\beta_{j+1},{\bf a}_{j+1}},\operatorname{ev})\,.

The iterative hypothesis implies that
\mathbb{S}_{i}(e_{j+1})\simeq{\bm{L}}_{i}(e_{j+1})\simeq L_{\beta_{j+1},{\bf a}_{j+1}}
. Since
\Delta^{\mathfrak{w}}_{k}(e_{j})\simeq(e_{j};0)
for
k\in[1,j)
, we obtain the equivalence
\mathbb{S}_{i}(v_{j})\simeq\mathbb{S}_{j,i}(v_{j})
. In order to compute
\mathbb{S}_{j,i}(v_{j})
, note that (
79
) implies that
\mathbb{S}_{j+1,i}(v_{j})\simeq(v_{j};0,L_{\beta_{j+1},{\bf a}_{j+1}},0)
. Thus equivalence (
89
) implies

\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{j},\mathbb{S}_{j+1,i})\simeq\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w}}(v_{j})}((k[-1],0,0),(0,L_{\beta_{j+1},{\bf a}_{j+1}},0))\simeq\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{D}}(S_{p_{j}},L_{\beta_{j+1},{\bf a}_{j+1}})\,,

and therefore, together with the defining equation (
94
) for
\mathbb{S}_{j,i}
, we obtain

\mathbb{S}_{j,i}\simeq\operatorname{cof}(\Delta_{j}\otimes\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(S_{p_{j}},L_{\beta_{j+1},{\bf a}_{j+1}})\to\mathbb{S}_{j+1,i})\,.

(97)

Evaluating (
97
) at
v_{j}
leads to the equivalence

\mathbb{S}_{j,i}(v_{j})\simeq\operatorname{cof}((v_{j};\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(S_{p_{j}},L_{\beta_{j+1},{\bf a}_{j+1}})[-1],0,0)\to(v_{j};0,L_{\beta_{j+1},{\bf a}_{j+1}},0))\simeq{\bm{L}}_{i}(v_{j})\,.

Therefore
\mathbb{S}_{i}(v_{j})\simeq\mathbb{S}_{j,i}(v_{j})\simeq{\bm{L}}_{i}(v_{j})
, which proves (
96
) at the vertex
v_{j}
. The equivalence (
96
) at the edge
e_{j}
follows from that, as the map
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w}}(v_{j}\to e_{j})
provides the required equivalence. This concludes the proof of the equivalence
{\bm{L}}^{\mathfrak{w}}_{i}\simeq\mathbb{S}^{\mathfrak{w}}_{i}
in Part (1).

Part (1).B
. For the equivalence
{\bm{L}}^{\mathfrak{w}}_{i}\simeq\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}^{\mathfrak{w}}_{i}
in Part (1), the same iterative strategy works but the computations are necessarily different. In this case, the key part of the argument is still showing

{\bm{L}}_{i}(v_{j})\simeq\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{i}(v_{j})\qquad\mbox{ and }\qquad{\bm{L}}_{i}(e_{j})\simeq\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{i}(e_{j}),\quad\forall j\in[1,i),

(98)

as the cases
j\in[i,m]
are identical to the previous argument. The equivalence
\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{i}(v_{j})\simeq\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{j,i}(v_{j})
still holds, and now (
80
) implies
\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{j+1,i}(v_{j})\simeq(v_{j};\operatorname{Mor}(S_{p_{j}},L_{\beta_{j},{\bf a}_{j}}),L_{\beta_{j},{\bf a}_{j}},\operatorname{ev})
. The equivalence (
90
) implies

\operatorname{Mor}(\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{j+1,i},\nabla_{j})\simeq\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w}}(v_{j})}((\operatorname{Mor}(S_{p_{j}},L_{\beta,{\bf a}}),L_{\beta_{j+1},{\bf a}_{j+1}},\operatorname{ev}),(k[-1],0,0))\simeq\operatorname{Mor}(L_{\beta_{j+1},{\bf a}_{j+1}},S_{p_{j}})^{\ast}[-1]\,.

which, along with (
95
), leads to the equivalences

\displaystyle\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{j,i}

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{fib}(\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{j+1,i}\to\nabla_{j}\otimes\tau_{\leq 0}(\operatorname{Mor}(L_{\beta_{j+1},{\bf a}_{j+1}},S_{p_{j}})^{\ast}[-1]))

(99)

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{fib}(\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{j+1,i}\to\nabla_{j}\otimes(\tau_{\leq-1}\operatorname{Mor}(S_{p_{j}},L_{\beta_{j+1},{\bf a}_{j+1}}))[1])\,,

where the second equivalence uses the fact that
\mathcal{D}
is
2
-Calabi–Yau. Evaluating (
99
) at
v_{j}
leads to the equivalence

\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{j,i}(v_{j})\simeq\operatorname{fib}((v_{j};\operatorname{Mor}(S_{p_{j}},L_{\beta,{\bf a}}),L_{\beta,{\bf a}},\operatorname{ev})\to(v_{j};\tau_{\leq-1}\operatorname{Mor}(S_{p_{j}},L_{\beta,{\bf a}}),0,0))\simeq{\bm{L}}_{i}(v_{j})\,.

Hence
\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{i}(v_{j})\simeq\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{j,i}(v_{j})\simeq{\bm{L}}_{i}(v_{j})
, which proves (
98
) at the vertex
v_{j}
, and therefore also at the edges, as above. This concludes the proof of Part (1) in the statement. Part (2) follows from Part (1) and
Proposition

A.12
.∎

8.2
Invariance of categorical Lusztig cycles under weave equivalences

Let us now establish Theorem
B
.(3), see also
Theorem

4.5
. In order to do so, we first prove
Lemma

8.3
and
Proposition

8.4
, which will be used in the argument for Theorem
B
.(3).

Let
\mathfrak{w}
be a Demazure weave with
m
trivalent vertices and
{\bf G_{\mathfrak{w}}}
its associated graph. Given
i\in[1,m]
, consider the subgraph
{\bf G}_{\mathfrak{w},\geq i}\subset{\bf G}_{\mathfrak{w}}
form by the vertices
v_{i},\dots,v_{m}
and all their incident edges. In particular, the edge
e_{i}
becomes external in
{\bf G}_{\mathfrak{w},\geq i}
. Such a subgraph specifies the corresponding exit path subcategory
\operatorname{Exit}({\bf G}_{\mathfrak{w},\geq i})\subset\operatorname{Exit}({\bf G}_{\mathfrak{w}})
. The weave schober
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w}}
for
\mathfrak{w}
, which is a functor with domain
\operatorname{Exit}({\bf G}_{\mathfrak{w}})
, can consequently be restricted to this subcategory: the restriction of
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w}}
along
\operatorname{Exit}({\bf G}_{\mathfrak{w},\geq i})\subset\operatorname{Exit}({\bf G}_{\mathfrak{w}})
will be denoted by
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w},\geq i}
.

Lemma 8.3
.

Let
\mathfrak{w}
be a Demazure weave and
i\in[1,m]
. Then the following holds:

(1)

The restriction of coCartesian sections along the inclusion
\operatorname{Exit}({\bf G}_{\mathfrak{w},\geq i})\subset\operatorname{Exit}({\bf G}_{\mathfrak{w}})
defines a functor

\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}\colon R\Gamma({\bf G_{\mathfrak{w}}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w}})\to R\Gamma({\bf G}_{\mathfrak{w},\geq i},\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w},\geq i})\,.

In addition, the functor
\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}
admits a fully faithful left adjoint

\operatorname{ind}^{L}_{\geq i}\colon R\Gamma({\bf G}_{\mathfrak{w},\geq i},\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w},\geq i})\longrightarrow R\Gamma({\bf G_{\mathfrak{w}}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w}})\,.

(2)

For
j\in[i,m]
, the global section
\mathbb{S}_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{w}}
lies in the essential image of
\operatorname{ind}^{L}_{\geq i}
.

(3)

For
j\in[i,m]
, there exist equivalences

\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(\mathbb{S}_{j}^{\mathfrak{w}})\simeq\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(\mathbb{S}_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{w}}),\qquad\mbox{ and }\qquad\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{j}^{\mathfrak{w}})\simeq\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{w}})\,

in
R\Gamma({\bf G}_{\mathfrak{w},\geq i},\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w},\geq i})
.

(4)

For
j\in[i,m]
, there exists the following equivalence in
R\Gamma({\bf G}_{\mathfrak{w},\geq i},\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w},\geq i})
:

\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(\mathbb{S}_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{w}})\simeq\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{w}})

(100)

Proof.

Part (1) follows from inspecting the construction of
\operatorname{ind}^{L}_{\geq i}
in
[
Chr25b
, Lemma 4.20]
and using
[
Chr25b
, Prop. 4.18 & Rmk. 4.19]
. Part (2) follows from the definition of
\mathbb{S}_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{w}}
and the fact that each thimble
\Delta_{l}^{\mathfrak{w}}
, with
l\in[i,m]
, lies in the essential image of
\operatorname{ind}^{L}_{\geq i}
by (
89
). Part (3) follows from inspecting the definitions of
\mathbb{S}_{j}^{\mathfrak{w}},\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{j}^{\mathfrak{w}}
and the fact that
\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(\Delta_{l}^{\mathfrak{w}})\simeq\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(\nabla_{l}^{\mathfrak{w}})\simeq 0
for
l\in[1,i)
. Finally, Part (4) follows from Part (3) and the equivalence
\mathbb{S}_{j}^{\mathfrak{w}}\simeq\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{j}^{\mathfrak{w}}
, established in
Theorem

8.2
.
∎

Let us introduce another variant of
Definition

8.1
, closely related to (
94
) and (
95
). For that, consider
i\in[1,m]
and
j\in[i+2,m]
, and define

\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j}^{\mathfrak{w}}\coloneqq T^{+}_{\Delta_{i+1}^{\mathfrak{w}}}(\mathbb{S}_{i+2,j}^{\mathfrak{w}})\,.

(101)

where
T^{+}_{\Delta_{i+1}^{\mathfrak{w}}}
denotes the full twist along
{\Delta_{i+1}^{\mathfrak{w}}}
, not a semi-twist. Similarly, we define

\reflectbox{$\mathbb{Z}$}_{i+1,j}^{\mathfrak{w}}\coloneqq T^{-}_{\nabla_{i+1}^{\mathfrak{w}}}(\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{i+2,j}^{\mathfrak{w}})\,.

(102)

with
T^{-}_{\nabla_{i+1}^{\mathfrak{w}}}
denoting the full cotwist along
\nabla_{i+1}^{\mathfrak{w}}
. Before stating the next result, relating
Equation

94
to
Equation

101
and
Equation

95
to
Equation

102
,
we recall that if
\mathfrak{w}\to\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}
is a backward pulley move or backwards weave mutation, exchanging the heights of the vertices
p_{i}
and
p_{i+1}
, then
Theorem

3.8
provides an equivalence of global sections:

\mu_{i+1}^{\flat}\colon R\Gamma({\bf G_{\mathfrak{w}}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}R\Gamma({\bf G}_{\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}}),

see also
Remark

3.9
.

Proposition 8.4
.

Let
\mathfrak{w}
be a Demazure weave with
m
trivalent vertices,
i\in[1,m]
and
j\in(i+1,m]
. Then the following holds:

(1)

In the category
R\Gamma({\bf G_{\mathfrak{w}}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w}})
, there exists equivalences

\mathbb{S}_{i+1,j}^{\mathfrak{w}}\simeq T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i+1}^{\mathfrak{w}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j}^{\mathfrak{w}})

(103)

and similarly

\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{i+1,j}^{\mathfrak{w}}\simeq T^{+,\geq 0}_{\nabla_{i+1}^{\mathfrak{w}}}(\reflectbox{$\mathbb{Z}$}_{i+1,j}^{\mathfrak{w}}).

(104)

(2)

Let
\mathfrak{w}\to\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}
be a backward pulley move or backwards weave mutation, exchanging the heights of the vertices
p_{i},p_{i+1}
. Let

\phi\colon R\Gamma({\bf G}_{w^{\prime}},\mathcal{F}_{w^{\prime}})\simeq R\Gamma({\bf G_{\mathfrak{w}}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w}})

be the corresponding equivalence of global sections arising from
Theorem

3.8
. Then there exist equivalences in
R\Gamma({\bf G}_{\mathfrak{w},\geq i},\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w},\geq i})

\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j}^{\mathfrak{w}})\simeq\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}((\mu_{i+1}^{\flat})^{-1}(\reflectbox{$\mathbb{Z}$}_{i+1,j}^{\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}}))\,.

(105)

and

(\mu_{i+1}^{\flat})^{-1}(\mathbb{S}_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}})\simeq T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i+1}^{\mathfrak{w}}}T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i}^{\mathfrak{w}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j}^{\mathfrak{w}})\,.

(106)

Proof.

Let us start with (
103
) in Part (1), and again drop
\mathfrak{w}
from the notation for ease. By definition, the right hand side of (
103
) is

T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i+1}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j})=\operatorname{fib}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j}^{\mathfrak{w}}\longrightarrow\tau_{\leq 0}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j},\Delta_{i+1})}^{*}\otimes\Delta_{i+1})).

To compute the morphism object highlighted in blue above, first use the defining cofiber sequence (
101
)

\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i+1},\mathbb{S}_{i+2,j})\otimes\Delta_{i+1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\operatorname{ev}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{S}_{i+2,j}\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j}=\operatorname{cof}(\operatorname{ev})

(107)

and apply the functor
\operatorname{Mor}(\mathchar 45\relax,\Delta_{i+1}^{\mathfrak{w}})
to (
107
). Since
\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{k}^{\mathfrak{w}},\Delta_{i+1}^{\mathfrak{w}})\simeq 0
if
k\in(i+1,m]
, we obtain
\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{S}_{i+2,j},\Delta_{i+1}^{\mathfrak{w}})\simeq 0
and thus applying the functor
\operatorname{Mor}(\mathchar 45\relax,\Delta_{i+1}^{\mathfrak{w}})
to (
107
) yields the equivalence

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j},\Delta_{i+1})}\simeq\operatorname{Mor}(\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i+1},\mathbb{S}_{i+2,j})\otimes\Delta_{i+1}[1],\Delta_{i+1})\simeq{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i+1},\mathbb{S}_{i+2,j})^{*}[-1]}\,,

where we used
\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i+1},\Delta_{i+1})\simeq k
in the second equivalence above. In consequence, the right hand side of (
103
) can be expressed as

\displaystyle T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i+1}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{fib}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j}^{\mathfrak{w}}\longrightarrow\tau_{\leq 0}(({\color[rgb]{0,1,1}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i+1},\mathbb{S}_{i+2,j})^{*}[-1]})^{*})\otimes\Delta_{i+1})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{fib}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j}^{\mathfrak{w}}\longrightarrow\tau_{\leq 0}(\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i+1},\mathbb{S}_{i+2,j})[1])\otimes\Delta_{i+1})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{fib}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j}^{\mathfrak{w}}\longrightarrow\tau_{\leq-1}(\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i+1},\mathbb{S}_{i+2,j}))[1]\otimes\Delta_{i+1})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{cof}(\tau_{\geq 0}(\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i+1},\mathbb{S}_{i+2,j}))\otimes\Delta_{i+1})\longrightarrow\mathbb{S}_{i+2,j})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathbb{S}_{i+1,j}.

This establishes the equivalence (
103
). The equivalence (
104
) is proven similarly.

For Part (2), we ease notation by omitting
\mathfrak{w}
and
\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}
but denoting
\mathbb{S}_{i,j}^{{}^{\prime}}\coloneqq\mathbb{S}_{i,j}^{\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}}
and so on, i.e. a prime in the exponent indicates the object is associated to
\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}
, instead of
\mathfrak{w}
. We also omit the equivalence
(\mu_{i+1}^{\flat})^{-1}
in the notation, as the prime notation above already indicates where objects belong.

We prove the equivalence (
105
) as follows. First,
Lemma

7.22
.(3) implies
\Delta_{i+1}\simeq\Delta_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}
and thus, by
Lemma

8.3
.(1),
\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(\Delta_{i+1})\simeq\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(\Delta_{i}^{{}^{\prime}})
. Since
\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(\Delta^{{}^{\prime}}_{i})\simeq\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(\nabla_{i}^{{}^{\prime}})
, we obtain

\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(\Delta_{i+1})\simeq\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(\nabla_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}).

(108)

Second,
Lemma

7.22
.(3) also implies that
\Delta_{k}\simeq\Delta_{k}^{\prime}
if
k\in[i+2,m]
, and therefore

\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(\mathbb{S}_{i+2,j})\simeq\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(\mathbb{S}_{i+2,j}^{{}^{\prime}})\simeq\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(T^{-}_{\nabla_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}}T^{-}_{\nabla_{i+1}^{{}^{\prime}}}\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{i+2,j}^{{}^{\prime}})\,,

(109)

where the second equivalence in (
109
) follows from (
92
) restricted to
{\bf G}_{\mathfrak{w}^{\prime},\geq i}
. Third, the equivalence

\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(T^{+}_{\nabla_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}}T^{-}_{\nabla_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}})\simeq\mbox{id}

(110)

follows from the definitions of the adjoint twist and cotwist functors. Applying these equivalences yields

\displaystyle\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:partial_simples_full}}}{{=}}\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(T^{+}_{\Delta_{i+1}}(\mathbb{S}_{i+2,j}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:proof_thimble_restriction_above_i}}}{{\simeq}}\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(T^{+}_{\nabla_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}}(\mathbb{S}_{i+2,j}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:S_to_backS_above_i}}}{{\simeq}}\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(T^{+}_{\nabla_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}}T^{-}_{\nabla_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}}T^{-}_{\nabla_{i+1}^{{}^{\prime}}}(\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{i+2,j}^{{}^{\prime}}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:twist_cotwist_identity}}}{{\simeq}}\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(T^{-}_{\nabla_{i+1}^{{}^{\prime}}}(\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{i+2,j}^{{}^{\prime}}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:partial_cosimples_full}}}{{=}}\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(\reflectbox{$\mathbb{Z}$}_{i+1,j}^{{}^{\prime}})\,.

This establishes equivalence (
105
). In order to show equivalence (
106
), we first note that

\displaystyle\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(\mathbb{S}_{i,j}^{{}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:ssimpij_equal_csimpij_abovei}}}{{\simeq}}\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{i,j}^{{}^{\prime}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Zcsimp_equivalence}}}{{\simeq}}\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(T^{-,\leq 0}_{\nabla_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}}T^{+,\geq 0}_{\nabla_{i+1}^{{}^{\prime}}}(\reflectbox{$\mathbb{Z}$}_{i+1,j}^{{}^{\prime}}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:proof_thimble_restriction_above_i}}}{{\simeq}}\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i+1}}T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}(\reflectbox{$\mathbb{Z}$}_{i+1,j}^{{}^{\prime}}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:restriction_zssimp_is_zcsimp}}}{{\simeq}}\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i+1}}T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j}))\,,

where the second to last equivalence also used
\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(\nabla_{i+1}^{{}^{\prime}})\simeq\operatorname{res}_{\geq i}(\Delta_{i})
, which is implied by
Lemma

7.22
. Since both
\mathbb{S}_{i,j}^{{}^{\prime}}
and
T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i+1}}T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j})
lie in the image of
\operatorname{ind}^{L}_{\geq i}
, the equivalence (
106
) follows from the fact that the functor
\operatorname{ind}^{L}_{\geq i}
is fully faithful, cf.
Lemma

8.3
.(1).
∎

Proof of Theorem
B
.
(3)
.

To ease notation, we omit the equivalence of global sections of the weave schobers induced by the given weave equivalence
\mathfrak{w}\sim\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}
, cf.
Theorem

3.8
.(3).
By decomposing the given weave equivalence
\mathfrak{w}\simeq\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}
into height-preserving weave equivalences and pulley moves, it suffices to prove the assertion for these two types of weave equivalences. In the former case, if the weave equivalence
\mathfrak{w}\sim\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}
is height-preserving,
Lemma

7.22
implies that there is an identification of their standard weave thimbles
\Delta_{i}^{\mathfrak{w}}\simeq\Delta_{i}^{\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}}
for all
i\in[1,m]
. Hence, by construction, the objects of the simple minded collections
\mathbb{S}_{i}^{\mathfrak{w}}\simeq\mathbb{S}_{i}^{\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}}
are equivalent for all
i\in[1,m]
.
Theorem

8.2
thus implies that
{\bm{L}}_{i}^{\mathfrak{w}}\simeq{\bm{L}}_{i}^{\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}}
for all
i\in[1,m]
, concluding Theorem
B
.
(3)
for height-preserving equivalences. The case of a pulley move, which is more subtle, is proven as follows.

Consider a pulley move
\mathfrak{w}\to\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}
exchanging the heights of the vertices
p_{i},p_{i+1}
, for some
i\in[1,m]
. Swapping
\mathfrak{w}
and
\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}
if necessary, we can and do assume that the weave equivalence is a backward pulley move from
\mathfrak{w}
to
\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}
. As above, we ease notation by omitting
\mathfrak{w}
and
\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}
, keeping the prime as an exponent in the latter case. Since
Lemma

7.22
implies that
\Delta_{k}\simeq\Delta_{k}^{{}^{\prime}}
for all
k\in[1,i)
, we certainly have
\mathbb{S}_{k}\simeq\mathbb{S}_{k}^{{}^{\prime}}
, and thus
{\bm{L}}_{k}\simeq{\bm{L}}_{k}^{{}^{\prime}}
for all
k\in[1,i)
by
Theorem

8.2
. Our next task is showing that
{\bm{L}}_{j}\simeq{\bm{L}}_{j}^{{}^{\prime}}
for all
j\in[i,m]
.

For the cases
j\in\{i,i+1\}
, it suffices to show the equivalences

\mathbb{S}_{i,i+1}\simeq\mathbb{S}_{i,i}^{{}^{\prime}}\qquad\mbox{ and }\qquad\mathbb{S}_{i,i}\simeq\mathbb{S}_{i,i+1}^{{}^{\prime}}\,.

(111)

Indeed, since
\Delta_{k}\simeq\Delta_{k}^{{}^{\prime}}
for all
k\in[1,i)
, as already used above, the equivalences in (
111
) respectively imply
{\bm{L}}_{i+1}\simeq{\bm{L}}_{i+1}^{{}^{\prime}}
and
{\bm{L}}_{i}\simeq{\bm{L}}_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}
. To establish the first equivalence in (
111
), we use
Lemmas

5.4
and
7.17
to obtain the vanishing

\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i},\Delta_{i+1})\simeq\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{D}}(S_{p_{i}},S_{p_{i+1}})\simeq 0.

(112)

From such vanishing, it thus follows that

\mathbb{S}_{i,i+1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:partial_simples}}}{{\simeq}}T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}(\Delta_{i+1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:vanishing_standard_hom}}}{{\simeq}}\Delta_{i+1}\simeq\Delta_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:partial_simples}}}{{\simeq}}\mathbb{S}_{i,i}^{{}^{\prime}},

where we used
Lemma

7.22
to obtain
\Delta_{i+1}\simeq\Delta_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}
. For the second equivalence in (
111
), we proceed as follows. First, note that
Lemma

7.22
implies
\Delta_{i+1}^{{}^{\prime}}\simeq T^{-}_{\Delta_{i+1}}(\Delta_{i})
. Second, since
\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i+1},\Delta_{i}^{)}\simeq 0
by exceptionality, we obtain

T^{+}_{\Delta_{i+1}}(\Delta_{i+1}^{{}^{\prime}})\simeq T^{+}_{\Delta_{i+1}}T^{-}_{\Delta_{i+1}}(\Delta_{i})\simeq\Delta_{i}.

(113)

Third, the vanishing in (
112
) implies that

\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i+1},\Delta_{i+1}^{{}^{\prime}})\simeq\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i+1},T^{-}_{\Delta_{i+1}}(\Delta_{i}))\simeq\operatorname{fib}(\underbrace{\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i+1},\Delta_{i})}_{\simeq 0}\to\underbrace{\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i+1},\Delta_{i+1})}_{\simeq k}\otimes\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i},\Delta_{i+1})^{*})

is concentrated in positive degrees. Since
\Delta_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}=\Delta_{i+1}
by
Lemma

7.22
, this thus implies the equivalence

T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}}(\Delta_{i+1}^{{}^{\prime}})\simeq T^{+}_{\Delta_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}}(\Delta_{i+1}^{{}^{\prime}}).

(114)

In consequence, we obtain

\mathbb{S}_{i,i+1}^{{}^{\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:partial_simples}}}{{\simeq}}T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}}(\Delta_{i+1}^{{}^{\prime}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:semitwist_is_twist_case}}}{{\simeq}}T^{+}_{\Delta_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}}(\Delta_{i+1}^{{}^{\prime}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:equivalence_for_Liplusone}}}{{\simeq}}\Delta_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:partial_simples}}}{{\simeq}}\mathbb{S}_{i,i}\,.

(115)

Theorem

8.2
and (
111
), now established, thus imply
{\bm{L}}_{i}\simeq{\bm{L}}_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}
and
{\bm{L}}_{i+1}\simeq{\bm{L}}_{i+1}^{{}^{\prime}}
. The remaining task is establishing that
{\bm{L}}_{j}\simeq{\bm{L}}_{j}^{{}^{\prime}}
for all
j\in[i+2,m]
. For that it suffices to prove the equivalence

\mathbb{S}_{i,j}\simeq\mathbb{S}_{i,j}^{{}^{\prime}},\qquad\forall j\in[i+2,m].

(116)

By (
103
) and (
106
), equivalence (
116
) is itself equivalent to

\framebox{$T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i+1}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j})\simeq T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i+1}}T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j})$}\qquad\forall j\in[i+2,m].

(117)

The remainder of the proof is to establish (
117
). For that, we will first prove the following two equivalences

\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i},\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j})\simeq\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i},T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i+1}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j}))

(118)

\tau_{\leq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j},\Delta_{i+1})^{*}\simeq\tau_{\leq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j}),\Delta_{i+1})^{*}

(119)

To prove (
118
), consider the following cofiber sequence, obtained by applying
\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i},\mathchar 45\relax)
to the defining fiber sequence for
T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i+1}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j})
:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i},\tau_{\leq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j},\Delta_{i+1})^{*}\otimes\Delta_{i+i})[-1]}\to\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i},T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i+1}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i},\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j})

(120)

The first term is equivalent to

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i},\tau_{\leq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j},\Delta_{i+1})^{*}\otimes\Delta_{i+i})[-1]}\simeq\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i},\Delta_{i+1})[-1]\otimes\tau_{\leq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j},\Delta_{i+1})^{*}

and thus (
112
) implies that such term is concentrated in degrees less equal than
-2
. Hence, the truncation
\tau_{\geq 0}(f)
of the morphism
f
in (
120
) is an equivalence, thus establishing equivalence (
118
).

To prove (
119
), we similarly consider the following cofiber sequence, obtained by applying
\operatorname{Mor}(\mathchar 45\relax,\Delta_{i+1})^{*}
to the defining cofiber sequence for
T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j})
:

\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j},\Delta_{i+1})^{*}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Mor}(T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j}),\Delta_{i+1})^{*}\to{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}\operatorname{Mor}(\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i},\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j})\otimes\Delta_{i},\Delta_{i+1})^{*}[1]}\,.

(121)

The third term is equivalent to

{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}\operatorname{Mor}(\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i},\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j})\otimes\Delta_{i},\Delta_{i+1})^{*}[1]}\simeq\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i},\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j})\otimes\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i},\Delta_{i+1})^{*}[1]

and (
112
) implies that it is concentrated in degrees greater equal than
2
. Therefore, the truncation
\tau_{\leq 0}(g)
of the morphism
g
in (
121
) is an equivalence. This establishes (
119
).

Finally, let us conclude the required equivalence (
117
) from (
118
) and (
119
). For that, consider the following commutative square, which is not biCartesian:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i},T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i+1}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j}))\otimes\Delta_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tau_{\leq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j}),\Delta_{i+1})^{*}\otimes\Delta_{i+1}}}

(122)

The equivalence (
118
), applied to the upper left corner of (
122
), implies that (
122
) is equivalent to

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i},\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j})\otimes\Delta_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tau_{\leq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j}),\Delta_{i+1})^{*}\otimes\Delta_{i+1}}}

(123)

By the definition of the semi-twist along
\Delta_{i}
, the horizontal cofiber of (
123
) yields the coevaluation morphism

T_{\Delta_{i}}^{+,\geq,0}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j})\longrightarrow{\tau_{\leq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j}),\Delta_{i+1})^{*}\otimes\Delta_{i+1}}.

By definition of the semi-twist along
\Delta_{i+1}
, the (vertical) fiber of such a morphism is precisely the right hand side of (
117
). That is, the right hand side of (
117
) is equivalent to the vertical fiber of the horizontal cofiber of (
123
).

Similarly, the equivalence (
119
), applied to the lower right corner of (
122
), implies that (
122
) is equivalent to the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i},T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i+1}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j}))\otimes\Delta_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tau_{\leq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j},\Delta_{i+1})^{*}\otimes\Delta_{i+1}}}

(124)

The vertical fiber of (
124
) gives the evaluation morphism

{\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i},T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i+1}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j}))\otimes\Delta_{i}}\longrightarrow T_{\Delta_{i+1}}^{-,\leq 0}(\mathbb{Z}_{i+1,j}).

The (horizontal) cofiber of such a morphism is precisely the left hand side of (
117
). That is, the left hand side of (
117
) is equivalent to the horizontal cofiber of the vertical fiber of (
123
).

Since the horizontal cofiber of the vertical fiber of a square in a stable
\infty
-category is equivalent to the vertical fiber of the horizontal cofiber of the same square, the computations above show that both sides of (
117
) must themselves be equivalent. This establishes (
117
), and thus the equivalence
{\bm{L}}_{j}\simeq{\bm{L}}_{j}^{{}^{\prime}}
between the categorical Lusztig cycles for
j\in[i+2,m]
. By considering the argument altogether, we obtain the equivalence
{\bm{L}}_{j}\simeq{\bm{L}}_{j}^{{}^{\prime}}
between the categorical Lusztig cycles for all
j\in[1,m]
, as required.
∎

8.3
Tilting of categorical Lusztig cycles under weave mutation

Let us now prove Theorem
B
.(4), cf. also
Theorem

4.8
. At a technical level, an important difference in the following proof of Theorem
B
.(4), in contrast to the proof of Theorem
B
.(3) in
Section

8.2
, is that equivalence (
117
) does not hold for a weave mutation. In a nutshell, under a weave mutation, the difference between the two terms in (
117
) is non-trivial and, as we will now establish, leads to the simple-minded collection of categorical Lusztig cycles undergoing a mutation.

Proof of Theorem
B
.
(4)
.

Let us prove the result for a backward weave mutation
\mathfrak{w}\to\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}
, exchanging the vertices
p_{i-1}
and
p_{i}
. The case of a forward weave mutation follows from it. As in the proofs above, we omit explicitly writing the equivalence
R\Gamma({\bf G_{\mathfrak{w}}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w}})\simeq R\Gamma({\bf G}_{\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}})
, and the superscripts
\mathfrak{w},\mathfrak{w}^{\prime}
, keeping the prime as an exponent for the latter case. By
Theorem

8.2
, our task is equivalent to showing that the backward mutation of the simple-minded collection
\{\mathbb{S}_{j}\}
is precisely
\{\mathbb{S}_{j}^{{}^{\prime}}\}
. Specifically, for each
j\in[1,m]
, we must show that

\mathbb{S}_{j}^{{}^{\prime}}\simeq\begin{cases}\mathbb{S}_{i}[-1]&\mbox{ if }j=i\,,\\
\operatorname{cof}(\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\mathbb{S}_{i},\mathbb{S}_{j})\otimes\mathbb{S}_{i}[-1]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\operatorname{ev}}}{{\to}}\mathbb{S}_{j})&\mbox{ if }j\neq i\,.\\
\end{cases}

To ease notation, we abbreviate
\psi^{\flat}_{\mathbb{S}_{i}}(\mathbb{S}_{j})\coloneqq\operatorname{cof}(\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\mathbb{S}_{i},\mathbb{S}_{j})\otimes\mathbb{S}_{i}[-1]\to\mathbb{S}_{j})
. As in the proofs above, the cases
j\in[1,i)
are reasonably straightforward, the cases
j\in\{i-1,i\}
have more content, and the challenging cases are
j\in[i+1,m]
. Let us study each of these cases.

Case
j\in[1,i-1)
.
In this range,
Lemma

7.22
implies that
\mathbb{S}_{j}\simeq\mathbb{S}_{j}^{{}^{\prime}}
. By the local model of backward weave mutation, the Lusztig cycle
\gamma_{i}
ends at the vertex
p_{i-1}
, and therefore
\operatorname{ev}_{v_{l}}(\mathbb{S}_{i})\simeq 0
for all vertices with
l\in[1,i-1)
. Hence we obtain the vanishing
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\mathbb{S}_{i},\mathbb{S}_{j})\simeq 0
in this range, and thus

\psi^{\flat}_{\mathbb{S}_{i}}(\mathbb{S}_{j})\simeq\operatorname{cof}(0\otimes\mathbb{S}_{i}[-1]\to\mathbb{S}_{j})\simeq\mathbb{S}_{j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{lem:change_in_thimbles_under_weave_equivalence})}}{{\simeq}}\mathbb{S}_{j}^{{}^{\prime}},\qquad\forall j\in[1,i-1),

as we needed to show.

Case
j\in\{i-1,i\}
.
Let us start with
j=i-1
. In the local model for a backward weave mutation

\mathbb{S}_{i}\simeq\operatorname{cof}(\Delta_{i-1}\to\Delta_{i})\,.

(125)

Now, on the one hand, since
\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i},\mathbb{S}_{i-1})\simeq 0
, we obtain

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\mathbb{S}_{i},\mathbb{S}_{i-1})\simeq\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\Delta_{i-1}[1],\mathbb{S}_{i-1})\simeq k\,,

and thus

\psi^{\flat}_{\mathbb{S}_{i}}(\mathbb{S}_{i-1})\simeq\operatorname{cof}(\mathbb{S}_{i}[-1]\to\mathbb{S}_{i-1})\,.

(126)

On the other hand,
Lemma

7.22
implies
\Delta_{i-1}^{{}^{\prime}}\simeq\Delta_{i}
and so (
125
) leads to a cofiber sequence

\Delta_{i-1}\longrightarrow\Delta_{i-1}^{{}^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{S}_{i}\,.

(127)

Since
\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{j},\mathbb{S}_{i})\simeq 0
in the range
j\in[1,i-2)
, (
127
) implies the existence of a cofiber sequence

\mathbb{S}_{i-1}\longrightarrow\mathbb{S}_{i-1}^{{}^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{S}_{i}\,,

which itself implies
\mathbb{S}_{i-1}^{\prime}\simeq\operatorname{cof}(\mathbb{S}_{i}[-1]\to\mathbb{S}_{i-1})
. Therefore

\mathbb{S}_{i-1}^{\prime}\simeq\operatorname{cof}(\mathbb{S}_{i}[-1]\to\mathbb{S}_{i-1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:flat_tilting_iminus1}}}{{\simeq}}\psi^{\flat}_{\mathbb{S}_{i}}(\mathbb{S}_{i-1}),

concluding the case
j=i-1
. For the case
j=i
, we proceed as follows. First, consider the two sequences of equivalences

\operatorname{res}_{\geq i-1}(\Delta_{i-1})\simeq\operatorname{res}_{\geq i-1}(\nabla_{i-1})\simeq\operatorname{res}_{\geq i-1}(\nabla_{i}^{{}^{\prime}})

(128)

\operatorname{res}_{\geq i-1}(\Delta_{i})\simeq\operatorname{res}_{\geq i-1}(\Delta_{i-1}^{{}^{\prime}})\simeq\operatorname{res}_{\geq i-1}(\nabla_{i-1}^{{}^{\prime}})

(129)

which follow from
Lemma

8.3
and the definition of the weave thimbles, cf.
Definition

7.10
. Second, in the local model for a backward weave mutation we have

\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}\simeq\operatorname{fib}(\nabla_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}\to\nabla_{i-1}^{{}^{\prime}})\,.

(130)

From this, again using
Lemma

8.3
, we therefore obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}_{i}

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{ind}^{L}_{\geq i-1}\operatorname{res}_{\geq i-1}(\mathbb{S}_{i})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:tilting_simple_i}}}{{\simeq}}\operatorname{ind}^{L}_{\geq i-1}\operatorname{res}_{\geq i-1}(\operatorname{cof}(\Delta_{i-1}\to\Delta_{i}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:tilting_equivalences_res1}}}{{\simeq}}\operatorname{ind}^{L}_{\geq i-1}\operatorname{res}_{\geq i-1}(\operatorname{cof}(\nabla_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}\to\Delta_{i}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:tilting_equivalences_res2}}}{{\simeq}}\operatorname{ind}^{L}_{\geq i-1}\operatorname{res}_{\geq i-1}(\operatorname{cof}(\nabla_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}\to\nabla_{i-1}^{{}^{\prime}}))

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{ind}^{L}_{\geq i-1}\operatorname{res}_{\geq i-1}(\operatorname{fib}(\nabla_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}\to\nabla_{i-1}^{{}^{\prime}}))[1]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:tilting_equivalence_cosimplei}}}{{\simeq}}\operatorname{ind}^{L}_{\geq i-1}\operatorname{res}_{\geq i-1}(\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{i}^{{}^{\prime}})[1]

\displaystyle\simeq\mathbb{S}_{i}^{{}^{\prime}}[1].

This establishes the required result for
j\in\{i-1,i\}
.

Case
j\in(i,m]
.
We prove this case in two steps. The first step will be to establish the existence of a cofiber sequence

\framebox{$\mathbb{S}_{i}^{\oplus r}\longrightarrow\mathbb{S}_{j}\longrightarrow\mathbb{S}_{j}^{{}^{\prime}}$}

(131)

for some non-negative integer
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. The second step will be to show that

\framebox{$\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\mathbb{S}_{i},\mathbb{S}_{j})=r$}

(132)

In both cases, we will exhibit
r
as the rank of a linear map.

Proof of (
131
).
Let us focus on the first step, i.e. establishing the cofiber sequence (
131
). For that, let us first apply the functor
\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},\mathchar 45\relax)
to the defining fiber sequence for
T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j})
, and the functor
\operatorname{Mor}(\mathchar 45\relax,\Delta_{i})^{*}
to the defining cofiber sequence for
T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i-1}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j})
. The two resulting cofiber sequences read:

\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j}))\to\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},\mathbb{Z}_{i,j})\to{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},\tau_{\leq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j},\Delta_{i})^{*}\otimes\Delta_{i})}

(133)

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\operatorname{Mor}(\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},\mathbb{Z}_{i,j})\otimes\Delta_{i-1},\Delta_{i})^{*}}\to\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j},\Delta_{i})^{*}\to\operatorname{Mor}(T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i-1}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j}),\Delta_{i})^{*}\,.

(134)

The third term in (
133
), highlighted in orange, and the first term in (
134
), highlighted in purple can be expressed as

{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},\tau_{\leq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j},\Delta_{i})^{*}\otimes\Delta_{i})}\simeq\tau_{\leq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j},\Delta_{i})^{*}\otimes(k\oplus k[-2])\,,

(135)

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\operatorname{Mor}(\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},\mathbb{Z}_{i,j})\otimes\Delta_{i-1},\Delta_{i})^{*}}\simeq\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},\mathbb{Z}_{i,j})\otimes(k\oplus k[2])\,.

(136)

Combining (
133
) and (
135
), resp. (
134
) and (
136
), we obtain the two equivalences

\tau_{\geq 1}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i},T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j}))\simeq\tau_{\geq 1}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},\mathbb{Z}_{i,j})\,,

(137)

\tau_{\leq-1}\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j},\Delta_{i})^{*}\simeq\tau_{\leq-1}\operatorname{Mor}(T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i-1}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j}),\Delta_{i})^{*}\,.

(138)

In particular, considering the cohomologies of (
133
) and (
134
) in degree 0, and using (
137
) and (
138
) above, we obtain the following two exact sequence of vector spaces:

0\to\operatorname{Ext}^{0}(\Delta_{i-1},T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j}))\to\operatorname{Ext}^{0}(\Delta_{i-1},\mathbb{Z}_{i,j})\xrightarrow{\phi}\operatorname{Ext}^{0}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j},\Delta_{i})^{*}\,,

(139)

\operatorname{Ext}^{0}(\Delta_{i-1},\mathbb{Z}_{i,j})\xrightarrow{\phi}\operatorname{Ext}^{0}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j},\Delta_{i})^{*}\to\operatorname{Ext}^{0}(T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i-1}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j}),\Delta_{i})^{*}\to 0\,.

(140)

Note that the two morphisms labeled
\phi
in (
139
) and (
140
) are the same morphism of
k
-vector spaces. Let
r\coloneqq\operatorname{rk}(\phi)
be the rank of such a morphism, i.e. the dimension of its image in
\operatorname{Ext}^{0}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j},\Delta_{i})^{*}
. The next step to obtain the cofiber sequence (
131
) is to consider the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{i-1}^{\oplus r}[-1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{i}^{\oplus r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j}))\otimes\Delta_{i-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{i,j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tau_{\leq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j},\Delta_{i})^{*}\otimes\Delta_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},\mathbb{Z}_{i,j})\otimes\Delta_{i-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{i,j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tau_{\leq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i-1}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j}),\Delta_{i})^{*}\otimes\Delta_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{2}}

(141)

The columns of (
141
) are cofiber sequences, whereas horizontally each row is a 3-term coherent chain complex in
R\Gamma({\bf G_{\mathfrak{w}}},\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w}})
. Specifically, the first column of (
141
) is obtained from the cofiber sequence (
133
) by truncating with
\tau_{\geq 0}
, tensoring with
\Delta_{i-1}
, and using
\phi
as defined by (
139
). The third column of (
141
) is obtained from the cofiber sequence (
134
) by truncating with
\tau_{\leq 0}
, tensoring with
\Delta_{i}
, and using
\phi
as in (
140
). The second column should be self-explanatory. Note that by definition of the semi-twists

\operatorname{fib}(g_{2})\simeq T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j})\,,

(142)

\operatorname{cof}(h_{1})\simeq T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i-1}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j})\,.

(143)

Consider the cofiber sequence obtained by taking the fibers of the maps from the second column of (
141
) to its third column, and then the cofibers of the natural maps from the first column of (
141
) to the resulting such fibers. Intrinsically, this is the functorial cofiber totalization of (
141
), shifted by
[-1]
, understood as a fiber and cofibers sequence of 3-term coherent chain complexes.Explicitly, each term of this new cofiber sequence, corresponding to rows one, two and three respectively, is computed as follows.

\operatorname{cof}({\Delta_{i-1}^{\oplus r}[-1]}\longrightarrow\operatorname{fib}(f_{2}))\simeq\operatorname{cof}({\Delta_{i-1}^{\oplus r}[-1]}\longrightarrow\Delta_{i}^{\oplus r}[-1])\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:tilting_simple_i}}}{{\simeq}}{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\mathbb{S}_{i}^{\oplus r}[-1]}\,,

(144)

\displaystyle\operatorname{cof}(\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j}))\otimes\Delta_{i-1}\to\operatorname{fib}(g_{2}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:tilting_proof_totalization_preterm1}}}{{\simeq}}

(145)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:tilting_proof_totalization_preterm1}}}{{\simeq}}\operatorname{cof}(\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j}))\otimes\Delta_{i-1}\to T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j}))

\displaystyle\simeq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i-1}}T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j})}\,,

\displaystyle\operatorname{cof}(\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},\mathbb{Z}_{i,j})\otimes\Delta_{i-1}\to\operatorname{fib}(h_{2}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:tilting_proof_totalization_preterm2}}}{{\simeq}}

(146)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:tilting_proof_totalization_preterm2}}}{{\simeq}}\operatorname{cof}(\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},\mathbb{Z}_{i,j})\otimes\Delta_{i-1}\to T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i-1}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j}))

\displaystyle\simeq{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i-1}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j})}\,.

Therefore, from the horizontal (shifted) totalization of (
141
), and (
144
), (
145
) and (
146
), we obtain a cofiber sequence

{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\mathbb{S}_{i}^{\oplus r}[-1]}\longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i-1}}T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j})}\longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i-1}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j})}\,.

(147)

Now, equivalences (
103
) and (
106
) in
Proposition

8.4
respectively imply the equivalences

\mathbb{S}_{i-1,j}\simeq{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i-1}}T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j})}\,,

(148)

\mathbb{S}_{i-1,j}^{{}^{\prime}}\simeq{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}T^{+,\geq 0}_{\Delta_{i-1}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j})}\,.

(149)

Therefore (
147
) is equivalent to

{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\mathbb{S}_{i}^{\oplus r}[-1]}\longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathbb{S}_{i-1,j}}\longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,1,1}\mathbb{S}_{i-1,j}^{{}^{\prime}}}\,.

(150)

Since
\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{l},\mathbb{S}_{i})\simeq 0
for all
l\in[1,i-1)
, applying
T_{\Delta_{1}}^{+,\geq 0}\cdots T_{\Delta_{i-2}}^{+,\geq 0}
to the cofiber sequence (
150
) induces a cofiber sequence

(\mathbb{S}_{i})^{\oplus r}[-1]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varepsilon}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{S}_{j}\longrightarrow\mathbb{S}_{j}^{{}^{\prime}}\,.

This thus proves the existence of the cofiber sequence (
131
), and concludes the first step of the proof in this case
j\in(i,m]
.

Proof of (
132
).
Let us focus on the second step, i.e. proving
\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\mathbb{S}_{i},\mathbb{S}_{j})=r
, cf. (
132
), where
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
is as in the cofiber sequence (
131
). By the proof of (
131
) above, such
r=\operatorname{rk}(\phi)
is the rank of the morphism

\operatorname{Ext}^{0}(\Delta_{i-1},\mathbb{Z}_{i,j})\xrightarrow{\phi}\operatorname{Ext}^{0}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j},\Delta_{i})^{*}

(151)

featuring in (
139
) and (
140
). In order to compute
\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\mathbb{S}_{i},\mathbb{S}_{j})
, we first study the object
\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{S}_{i},\mathbb{S}_{j})
. By (
125
),

\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{S}_{i},\mathbb{S}_{j})\simeq\operatorname{fib}({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i},\mathbb{S}_{j})}\to{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},\mathbb{S}_{j})})

(152)

The first term in (
152
), highlighted in orange, can be described as

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i},\mathbb{S}_{j})}

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i},\mathbb{S}_{i,j})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:Zssimp_equivalence}}}{{\simeq}}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i},T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j}))

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{fib}(\underbrace{\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i},\mathbb{Z}_{i,j})}_{\simeq 0}\to\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i},\tau_{\leq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j},\Delta_{i})^{*}\otimes\Delta_{i}))

\displaystyle\simeq{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\tau_{\leq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j},\Delta_{i})^{*}[-1]}

since
\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i},\Delta_{i})\simeq k
. The second term in (
152
), highlighted in purple, is equivalent to

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},\mathbb{S}_{j})}

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},\mathbb{S}_{i-1,j})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:equivalence1_case_higherj}}}{{\simeq}}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},T_{\Delta_{i-1}}^{+,\geq 0}T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j}))

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{cof}(\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j}))\otimes\Delta_{i-1})\to\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j})))

\displaystyle\simeq\tau_{<0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},T^{-,\leq 0}_{\Delta_{i}}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j}))

\displaystyle\simeq\tau_{<0}\operatorname{fib}(\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},\mathbb{Z}_{i,j})\to\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},\tau_{\leq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j},\Delta_{i})^{*}\otimes\Delta_{i}))

\displaystyle\simeq{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\tau_{<0}\operatorname{fib}(\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},\mathbb{Z}_{i,j})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\Phi,\Theta)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\tau_{\leq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j},\Delta_{i})^{*}\otimes(k\oplus k[-2]))}

since
\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},\Delta_{i})\simeq k\oplus k[-2]
in the local model of a backward weave mutation. By construction, in the last term of the equivalences above, the morphism

\Phi:\operatorname{fib}(\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},\mathbb{Z}_{i,j})\longrightarrow\tau_{\leq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j},\Delta_{i})^{*},

cohomologically restricts in degree 0 to the morphism
\phi
in (
151
), i.e.
\operatorname{Ext}^{0}(\Phi)=\phi
, while
\Theta
denotes another morphism, to the component tensored with
k[-2]
.

Finally, by (
152
) and these two sequences of equivalences above, the object
\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{S}_{i},\mathbb{S}_{j})
is the fiber of a morphism

{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\tau_{\leq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j},\Delta_{i})^{*}[-1]}\to{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\tau_{<0}\operatorname{fib}(\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{i-1},\mathbb{Z}_{i,j})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\Phi,\Theta)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\tau_{\leq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{Z}_{i,j},\Delta_{i})^{*}\otimes(k\oplus k[-2]))}

(153)

By taking the homology of (
153
) in degree
-1
, i.e. cohomologically in degree
1
, we thus conclude that

\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\mathbb{S}_{i},\mathbb{S}_{j})=\operatorname{dim}_{k}(H_{-1}(\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{S}_{i},\mathbb{S}_{j})))=\operatorname{rk}(\operatorname{Ext}^{0}(\Phi))=\operatorname{rk}(\phi)=r,

which indeed establishes (
132
).

Conclusion of proof for case
j\in(i,m]
.
Since
\mathbb{S}_{j}^{{}^{\prime}}
is indecomposable, and (
132
) established that
\operatorname{dim}_{k}\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\mathbb{S}_{i},\mathbb{S}_{j})=r
, the morphism
\varepsilon
in (
150
), i.e. the left morphism of (
131
), must agree with the evaluation morphism

\mathbb{S}_{i}\otimes\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\mathbb{S}_{i},\mathbb{S}_{j})[-1]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\operatorname{ev}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{S}_{j}.

Therefore,
\mathbb{S}_{j}^{{}^{\prime}}\simeq\operatorname{cof}(\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\mathbb{S}_{i},\mathbb{S}_{j})\otimes\mathbb{S}_{i}[-1]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\operatorname{ev}}}{{\to}}\mathbb{S}_{j})
for all
j\in(i,m]
. This concludes the case
j\in(i,m]
and thus the proof of Theorem
B
.
(4)
.
∎

9
Silting objects and their weave cycles

In this section we conclude our results on categorical weave calculus by studying two silting collections
\{\mathbb{P}_{i}^{\mathfrak{w}}\}
and
\{\reflectbox{$\mathbb{P}$}_{i}^{\mathfrak{w}}\}
, respectively left and right dual to the simple-minded categorical Lusztig cycles
\{\bm{L}^{\mathfrak{w}}_{i}\}
. The goal is to describe the objects of these silting collections in terms of both the weave
\mathfrak{w}
and its perverse schober
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w}}
. In order to achieve this, we first prove a type of Ringel duality for weave schobers in
Section

9.1
, cf.
Proposition

9.5
. The silting collections are then introduced in
Section

9.2
, and we then use such duality to establish the desired characterizations in
Section

9.3
, cf.
Theorem

9.13
and
Corollary

9.14
.

9.1
Two-sided weave schobers

Let
\mathfrak{w}
be a Demazure weave with
m
trivalent vertices. In this subsection we first introduce three ribbon graphs
{\bf G}_{\mathfrak{w}}^{\downarrow}
,
{\bf G}_{\mathfrak{w}}^{\updownarrow}
and
{\bf G}_{\mathfrak{w}}^{\uparrow}
, and three corresponding perverse schobers
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w}}^{\downarrow}
,
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w}}^{\updownarrow}
and
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w}}^{\uparrow}
.

First,
{\bf G}_{\mathfrak{w}}^{\downarrow}\coloneqq{\bf G}_{\mathfrak{w}}
is defined to be the ribbon graph
{\bf G}_{\mathfrak{w}}
of
\mathfrak{w}
introduced in
Section

3
. By definition, the ribbon graph
{\bf G}_{\mathfrak{w}}^{\updownarrow}
is obtained from
{\bf G}_{\mathfrak{w}}^{\downarrow}
by adding an external edge
e_{\infty}
incident to the top vertex
v_{m}
. By definition, the ribbon graph
{\bf G}_{\mathfrak{w}}^{\uparrow}
is obtained from
{\bf G}_{\mathfrak{w}}^{\updownarrow}
by removing the external edge
e_{1}
incident to
v_{1}
. Second, their associated perverse schobers are defined using
2.10
as follows:

Definition 9.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{w}
be a Demazure weave with trivalent vertices
p_{1},\dots,p_{m}
. Then
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w}}^{\updownarrow}
, resp.
\mathcal{F}_{\mathfrak{w}}^{\uparrow}
, is the

-parametrized,
