A
. The middle and right columns also apply to (enhanced) triangulated categories, whereas the left column is strictly restricted to abelian categories.

In the above table, the index
i\in[1,m]
always ranges from
1
to
m
,
T_{Y}^{+,\geq 0}
,
T_{Y}^{-,>0}
and
T_{Y}^{+,<0}
denote semi-twists and
T^{+}
a twist,
\Delta_{i}^{\uparrow}
denote the exceptional collection in the Ringel-type dual category, and all the exceptional and co-exceptional collections are always assumed to be full. Note also that technically we build two simple-minded collections
\{\mathbb{S}_{i}\}
and
\{\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{i}\}
, but we prove in
Lemma

A.22
that
\{\mathbb{S}_{i}\}=\{\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{i}\}
.

Remark 9.17
.

One can show that any proper
k
-linear stable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
with a full exceptional collection
\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m}\in\mathcal{C}
arises formally as the
\infty
-category of global sections of a perverse schober parametrized by a linear ribbon graph with one external edge, as follows. First, there is a functor
\mathcal{C}\to\hat{\mathcal{C}}
from
\mathcal{C}
into the proper
2
-Calabi–Yau completion
\hat{\mathcal{C}}
of
\mathcal{C}
, mapping
\Delta_{i}
to a
2
-spherical object
X_{i}
. Then, we can use the ordered collection of spherical objects
X_{1},\dots,X_{m}\in\hat{\mathcal{C}}
to define a perverse schober
\mathcal{F}
, in line with
Definition

3.3
. The
\infty
-category of global sections of
\mathcal{F}
is then equivalent to
\mathcal{C}
in such a manner that the
i
th exceptional object
\Delta_{i}
is identified with the
i
th standard exceptional object defined using left induction as in
Equation

89
. See also
[
Chr26a
]
for more details, and note that this passage to the Calabi–Yau completion can be seen as an algebraic version of the passage to the double branched cover of a Lefschetz fibration in
[
Sei08
, Section (18a)]
.∎

Each of the notions of an exceptional, a simple-minded, or a silting collection admits a notion of mutation, cf.
Section

A.1
. For the latter two, these are part of tilting and silting theory, respectively. Now, a central result in the study of the tilting theory of simple-minded collections and the silting theory of silting collections is that Koszul duality commutes with these respective notions of mutation, see
Theorem

A.9
. The relation between mutations of a given full exceptional collection and mutations of the corresponding simple-minded collection, or the corresponding silting collection, is more subtle, cf.
Theorem

4.5
and
Theorem

4.8
. Based on these two results in our main text, we now present two sufficiency homological criteria ensuring that an exceptional mutation induces either a simple-minded mutation, or that it induces no mutation of the corresponding simple-minded collection.

Theorem 9.18
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a proper
k
-linear stable
\infty
-category with a full exceptional collection
\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m}
, and
i\in[1,m]
a fixed index. Suppose that

\operatorname{Ext}^{0}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Delta_{i},\Delta_{i+1})\simeq k\,,

\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{C}}^{l}(\Delta_{j},\operatorname{cof}(\Delta_{i}\to\Delta_{i+1}))\simeq 0\,,\qquad\forall l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0},j\in[1,i)\,,

as well as

\operatorname{Ext}^{-k}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Delta_{i},\Delta_{i+1})\simeq 0,\qquad\forall k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\,.

Then the associated simple-minded collection
\{\mathbb{S}_{j}\}_{1\leq j\leq m}
undergoes a backwards mutation at
\mathbb{S}_{i}
under a right mutation of the exceptional collection at the pair
(\Delta_{i},\Delta_{i+1})
.

Proof.

By
Remark

9.17
, we may assume that
\mathcal{C}
arises as the global sections of a perverse schober on a linear graph with
m
vertices and one external edge arising from a collection of
m
many
2
-spherical objects, and such that the full exceptional collection arises as the standard objects obtained from left induction. Then the proof of
Theorem

4.5
, see
Section

8.3
, applies (with only cosmetic changes) to prove the assertion.
∎

Via a similar argument, the proof of
Theorem

4.8
implies the following:

Theorem 9.19
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a proper
k
-linear stable
\infty
-category with a full exceptional collection
\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m}
, and
i\in[1,m]
a fixed index. Suppose that

\operatorname{Ext}^{-k}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Delta_{i},\Delta_{i+1})\simeq 0,\qquad\forall k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}.

Then, up to permutation, the associated simple-minded collection
\{\mathbb{S}_{i}\}
is invariant under a right mutation of the exceptional collection at
(\Delta_{i},\Delta_{i+1})
.

Appendix A
Exceptional, simple-minded, and silting collections in enhanced triangulated categories

The object of this appendix is to study simple-minded and silting collection in
k
-linear stable
\infty
-categories arising from full exceptional collections. Specifically, in
Section

A.1
we recall the notions of exceptional collections, simple-minded collections, and silting collections, as well as their corresponding mutations. In
Section

A.2
, we describe how a full exceptional collection in a
k
-linear stable
\infty
-category gives rise to Koszul dual simple-minded and silting collections. In
Section

A.3
, we discuss how our constructions generalize aspects of the theory of highest weight abelian categories. We refer to
Section

9.4
for a discussion on how the constructions in the main part of the manuscript relate to those in this appendix and the theory of highest weight abelian categories.

Throughout this section, we formulate all notions and statements in the setting of
k
-linear
\infty
-categories, to keep the notation consistent with the main text. The results can equally be formulated on the level of the
k
-linear triangulated homotopy
1
-categories
13
13

13

For instance, the notion of a
t
-structure on a stable
\infty
-category amounts exactly to a
t
-structure on the triangulated homotopy
1
-category.
However, some of the proofs make use of the enhancement, specifically when they appeal to Koszul duality, see
Theorem

A.9
.

A.1
Recollections

The objects of interest in this appendix are defined as follows.

Definition A.1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a small
k
-linear stable
\infty
-category.

(1)

A finite sequence of objects
\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m}
in
\mathcal{C}
is called an exceptional collection if

•

\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{D}}(\Delta_{i},\Delta_{i})\simeq k
for all
1\leq i\leq m
, and

•

\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{D}}(\Delta_{i},\Delta_{j})\simeq 0
for all
1\leq j<i\leq m
.

The exceptional collection is said to be a full exceptional collection if in addition

•

the object
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{m}\Delta_{i}
generates
\mathcal{C}
under finite limits and colimits.

(2)

A finite sequence of objects
\nabla_{1},\dots,\nabla_{m}
in
\mathcal{C}
is called a (full) co-exceptional collection if
\nabla_{m},\dots,\nabla_{1}
is a (full) exceptional collection.

(3)

A full exceptional collection
\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m}
and a full co-exceptional collection
\nabla_{m},\dots,\nabla_{1}
in
\mathcal{C}
are said to be dual to each other if

\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Delta_{i},\nabla_{j})\simeq\begin{cases}k&i=j,\\
0&\text{else},\end{cases}\,\qquad\forall i,j\in[1,m].

(4)

A finite collection of indecomposable objects
S_{1},\dots,S_{m}
in
\mathcal{C}
is called a simple-minded collection if

•

\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(S_{i},S_{i})
is coconnective, i.e.
\operatorname{Ext}^{l}(S_{i},S_{i})\simeq 0
for all
l<0
, for all
1\leq i\leq m
,

•

\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(S_{i},S_{j})[1]
is coconnective, i.e.
\operatorname{Ext}^{l}(S_{i},S_{j})\simeq 0
for all
l\leq 0
, for all
1\leq i,j\leq m
with
i\not=j
, and

•

the object
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{m}S_{i}
generates
\mathcal{C}
under finite limits and colimits as well as direct summands.

(5)

A finite collection of distinct indecomposable objects
P_{1},\dots,P_{m}
in
\mathcal{C}
is called a silting collection if
P=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{m}P_{i}
is a silting object, that is:

•

\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(P,P)
is connective, i.e.
\operatorname{Ext}^{i}(P,P)\simeq 0
for all
i>0
,

•

P
generates
\mathcal{C}
under finite limits and colimits and direct summands.

We note that the notion of a silting object is self-dual, i.e.
P
is a silting object in
\mathcal{C}
if and only if it is a silting object in
\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{op}}
.

(6)

A simple-minded collection
S_{1},\dots,S_{m}
in
\mathcal{C}
is said to be Koszul dual to a silting collection
P_{1},\dots,P_{m}
if

\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(P_{i},S_{i})\simeq\begin{cases}k&i=j,\\
0&\text{else},\end{cases}\qquad\forall i,j\in[1,m].

∎

For
Definition

A.1
.(6), we remark that a simple-minded collection is uniquely determined by being Koszul dual to a given silting collection and vice versa.

Remark A.2
.

(1)

Recall that a small
k
-linear stable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
is called proper if, for all objects
X,Y\in\mathcal{C}
, the derived Hom object
\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(X,Y)\in\mathcal{D}(k)
has finite dimensional total homology, i.e. lies in
\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(k)
. In this appendix, we will only study full exceptional collections in proper
k
-linear stable
\infty
-categories.

(2)

One can show that if a proper
k
-linear stable
\infty
-category admits a full exceptional collection, then it is also idempotent complete and smooth.∎

Exceptional collections, simple-minded collections and silting collections each admits a notion of mutation, as follows. (See also
[
KY14
, Section 7]
.) We begin by recalling the mutation of exceptional collections, which requires the notion of a twist.

Definition A.3

(Twists)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a proper
k
-linear stable
\infty
-category and
\Delta_{i-1},\Delta_{i},\Delta_{i+1}\in\mathcal{C}
.

(1)

The positive twist of
\Delta_{i}
by
\Delta_{i-1}
is defined as

T_{\Delta_{i-1}}^{+}(\Delta_{i})\coloneqq\operatorname{cof}(\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Delta_{i-1},\Delta_{i})\otimes\Delta_{i-1}\longrightarrow\Delta_{i})\,,

where the morphism is given by the evaluation map.

(2)

The negative twist of
\Delta_{i}
by
\Delta_{i+1}
is defined as

T_{\Delta_{i+1}}^{-}(\Delta_{i})\coloneqq\operatorname{fib}(\Delta_{i}\longrightarrow\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Delta_{i},\Delta_{i+1})^{*}\otimes\Delta_{i+1})\,,

where the morphism is given by the coevaluation map.

∎

Lemma A.4
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a proper
k
-linear stable
\infty
-category. Let
\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m}
be a full exceptional collection and
\nabla_{1},\dots,\nabla_{m}
a full co-exceptional collection in
\mathcal{C}
.

(1)

Fix
i\in[2,m]
. Then the objects

\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{i-2},T_{\Delta_{i-1}}^{+}(\Delta_{i}),\Delta_{i-1},\Delta_{i+1},\dots,\Delta_{m}

form an exceptional collection, called the forward or left mutation at the pair
(\Delta_{i-1},\Delta_{i})
.
Similarly, the objects

\nabla_{1},\dots,\nabla_{i-2},\nabla_{i},T^{+}_{\nabla_{i}}(\nabla_{i-1}),\nabla_{i+1},\dots,\nabla_{m}

form a co-exceptional collection, called the forward mutation at
(\nabla_{i-1},\nabla_{i})
.

(2)

Fix
i\in[1,m-1]
. Then the objects

\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{i-1},\Delta_{i+1},T_{\Delta_{i+1}}^{-}(\Delta_{i}),\Delta_{i+2},\dots,\Delta_{m}

(199)

form an exceptional collection, called the backward or right mutation at
(\Delta_{i},\Delta_{i+1})
.
Similarly, the objects

\nabla_{1},\dots,\nabla_{i-1},T^{-}_{\nabla_{i}}(\nabla_{i+1}),\nabla_{i},\nabla_{i+2},,\dots,\nabla_{m}

form a co-exceptional collection, called the backward or right mutation at
(\nabla_{i},\nabla_{i+1})
.∎

Remark A.5
.

If
\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m}
is a full exceptional collection in a proper
k
-linear stable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
, then there exists a unique dual full co-exceptional collection
\nabla_{1},\dots,\nabla_{m}
, given by

\nabla_{i}=T_{\Delta_{1}}^{+}T_{\Delta_{2}}^{+}\cdots T_{\Delta_{i-1}}^{+}(\Delta_{i})\,,

(200)

see for instance
[
Sei08
, Section 5k]
.∎

For mutations of simple-minded collections, we proceed as follows. First, fixing a simple-minded collection
S_{1},\dots,S_{m}
in
\mathcal{C}
in a
k
-linear stable
\infty
-category, it determines a bounded
t
-structure on
\mathcal{C}
whose heart
H
is a finite length abelian category, see for instance
[
Sch
, Theorem 4.4]
. Let us assume that the trianguled homotopy category of
\mathcal{C}
is also
\operatorname{Hom}
-finite and Krull–Schmidt. Then the object
S_{i}
in the simple-minded collection
determines torsion pairs
14
14

14

Recall that a torsion pair
\langle F,T\rangle
in an abelian category
A
consists of two full subcategories
F,T\subset A
, such that
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(Y,X)=0
for all
X\in F
and
Y\in T
, and for every
Z\in A
, there exists a short exact sequence
X\to Z\to Y
with
X\in F
and
Y\in T
.

\langle\operatorname{add}(S_{i}),T\rangle
and
\langle F,\operatorname{add}(S_{i})\rangle
, with
\operatorname{add}(S_{i})
the additive closure of
S_{i}
, see
[
Dug15
, Theorem 3.3]
.

The forward tilt
H^{\sharp}
of
H
at
\operatorname{add}(S_{i})
is defined as the abelian subcategory of
\mathcal{C}
arising from the torsion pair
\langle T,\operatorname{add}(S_{i})[1]\rangle
. Similarly, the backward tilt
H^{\flat}
of
H
is defined as the abelian subcategory of
\mathcal{C}
arising from the torsion pair
\langle\operatorname{add}(S_{i})[-1],F\rangle
.

Definition A.6

(Simple-minded mutation)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a
k
-linear stable
\infty
-category whose triangulated homotopy category is
\operatorname{Hom}
-finite and Krull–Schmidt. Let
S_{1},\dots,S_{m}
be a simple-minded collection in
\mathcal{C}
describing the simple objects in a heart
H\subset\mathcal{C}
. The forward mutation of
S_{1},\dots,S_{m}
is defined as the collection of simple objects in the forward tilt
H^{\sharp}
, considered as objects of
\mathcal{C}
. The backwards mutation of
S_{1},\dots,S_{m}
is similarly defined as the simple objects in the backward tilt
H^{\flat}
.∎

The following result provides a more explicit description of the tilts at an object
S_{i}\in\mathcal{C}
of a simple-minded collection, as in
Definition

A.6
, in the case that the object
S_{i}
is itself rigid.

Proposition A.7

(
[
KQ15
, Prop. 5.4]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a
k
-linear stable
\infty
-category whose triangulated homotopy category is
\operatorname{Hom}
-finite and Krull–Schmidt and
\{S_{j}\}_{1\leq j\leq m}
a simple-minded collection in
\mathcal{C}
. Fix
i\in[1,m]
and suppose that the object
S_{i}
is rigid. Then the following holds:

(1)

The forward tilt of
\{S_{j}\}_{1\leq j\leq m}
at
S_{i}
is a simple-minded collection in
\mathcal{C}
and it is given by

\{S_{i}[1]\}\cup\{\psi^{\sharp}_{S_{i}}(S_{j})\}_{j\in[1,m],j\not=i}

where

\psi^{\sharp}_{S_{i}}(S_{j})\coloneqq\operatorname{fib}(S_{j}\to\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(S_{j},S_{i})^{*}\otimes S_{i}[1])\,.

(2)

The backwards tilt of
\{S_{j}\}_{1\leq j\leq m}
at
S_{i}
is a simple-minded collection in
\mathcal{C}
and it is given by

\{S_{i}[-1]\}\cup\{\psi^{\flat}_{S_{i}}(S_{j})\}_{j\in[1,m],j\not=i}

where

\psi^{\flat}_{S_{i}}(S_{j})\coloneqq\operatorname{cof}(\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(S_{i},S_{j})\otimes S_{i}[-1]\to S_{j})\,.

∎

The notion of mutation for silting collections is as follows:

Definition A.8

(
[
AI12
, Def. 2.30]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a
k
-linear stable
\infty
-category whose triangulated homotopy category is
\operatorname{Hom}
-finite and Krull–Schmidt. Let
P_{1},\dots,P_{m}\in\mathcal{C}
be a silting collection.

(1)

The forward mutation of
P_{1},\dots,P_{m}
at
P_{i}
is the silting collection

P_{1},\dots,P_{i-1},\mu^{+}(P_{i}),P_{i+1},\dots,P_{m}

where

\mu^{+}(P_{i})=\operatorname{cof}(P_{i}\to\bigoplus_{j\not=i,j=1}^{m}\operatorname{Ext}^{0}_{\mathcal{C}}(P_{i},P_{j})^{*}\otimes P_{j})

is the cofiber of a minimal left
\operatorname{add}(\{P_{j},j\not=i\})
-approximation.

(2)

The backward mutation of
P_{1},\dots,P_{m}
at
P_{i}
is the silting collection

P_{1},\dots,P_{i-1},\mu^{-}(P_{i}),P_{i+1},\dots,P_{m}

where

\mu^{-}(P_{i})=\operatorname{fib}(\bigoplus_{j\not=i,j=1}^{m}\operatorname{Ext}^{0}_{\mathcal{C}}(P_{j},P_{i})\otimes P_{j}\to P_{i})

is the fiber of a minimal left
\operatorname{add}(\{P_{j},j\not=i\})
-approximation.∎

The relation between these collections and their mutations above can be summarized as follows:

Theorem A.9

(Koszul-duality
[
KN
,
KY14
,
SY19
]
)
.

Let
A
be a connective dg algebra. Assume that the total homology of
A
is finite dimensional or that
A
is smooth and that
H_{i}(A)
is finite dimensional for all
i\geq 0
. Then there is bijective correspondence (called Koszul duality) between equivalence classes of

(1)

silting collections in
\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(A)
,

(2)

simple-minded collections in
\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{fin}}(A)
, and

(3)

bounded
t
-structures on
\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{fin}}(A)
with length heart.

Further, the bijection between (1) and (2) commutes with mutations, and the corresponding simple-minded collections are Koszul dual to the corresponding silting collections.

Note that if
A
in
Theorem

A.9
is finite dimensional and smooth, then
\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(A)=\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{fin}}(A)
.

A.2
From exceptional collections to simple-minded and silting collections

Let us now explain how to construct a simple-minded collection and a silting collection from a given exceptional collection. For that, we will use the following notion of semi-twists.

Definition A.10

(Semi-twists)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a proper
k
-linear stable
\infty
-category and
X,Y\in\mathcal{C}
.

(1)

The negative inverse semi-twist of
X
by
Y
is defined as the fiber

T^{-,\leq 0}_{Y}(X)\coloneqq\operatorname{fib}(X\to\tau_{\leq 0}(\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(X,Y)^{*})\otimes Y)\,,

where
\tau_{\leq 0}(\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(X,Y)^{*})\subset\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(X,Y)^{*}
denotes the maximal summand concentrated in homologically negative degrees.

(2)

The (strictly) positive inverse semi-twist of
X
by
Y
is defined as the fiber

T^{-,>0}_{Y}(X)\coloneqq\operatorname{fib}(X\to\tau_{>0}(\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(X,Y)^{*})\otimes Y)\,,

where
\tau_{>0}(\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(X,Y)^{*})\subset\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(X,Y)^{*}
denotes the maximal summand concentrated in homologically strictly positive degrees.

(3)

The positive semi-twist of
X
by
Y
is defined as the cofiber

T^{+,\geq 0}_{Y}(X)\coloneqq\operatorname{cof}(\tau_{\geq 0}(\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(Y,X))\otimes Y\to X)\,.

(4)

The (strictly) negative semi-twist of
X
by
Y
is defined as the cofiber

T^{+,<0}_{Y}(X)\coloneqq\operatorname{cof}(\tau_{<0}(\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(Y,X))\otimes Y\to X)

∎

Remark A.11
.

The semi-twist functor
T^{+,\geq 0}_{Y}(\mathchar 45\relax)
in
Definition

A.10
.(3) also appears in
[
PYK23
, Appendix A]
, referred to as the truncated twist functor.∎

Our first result constructs a simple-minded collection from an exceptional collection:

Proposition A.12

(Simple-minded from exceptional)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a proper
k
-linear stable
\infty
-category.

(1)

Let
\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m}\in\mathcal{C}
be a full exceptional collection and define

\mathbb{S}_{i}\coloneqq T_{\Delta_{1}}^{+,\geq 0}(\cdots(T_{\Delta_{i-1}}^{+,\geq 0}(\Delta_{i})))\in\mathcal{C}\,.

Then the objects
\{\mathbb{S}_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq m}
form a simple-minded collection in
\mathcal{C}
.

(2)

Let
\nabla_{1},\dots,\nabla_{m}\in\mathcal{C}
be a full co-exceptional collection and define

\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{i}\coloneqq T_{\nabla_{1}}^{-,\leq 0}(\cdots(T_{\nabla_{i-1}}^{-,\leq 0}(\nabla_{i})))\in\mathcal{C}\,.

Then the objects
\{\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq m}
form a simple-minded collection in
\mathcal{C}
.

Every full exceptional collection determines a dual co-exceptional collection, and thus two associated simple-minded collections
\{\mathbb{S}_{i}\}
and
\{\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{i}\}
in
Proposition

A.12
. We show in
Lemma

A.22
below that these two simple-minded collections coincide.

Proof of
Proposition

A.12
.

Let us prove Part (1). Part (2) follows from the same computation in the opposite
\infty
-category. For Part (1), the objects
\{\mathbb{S}_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq m}
stably generate the exceptional collection, hence they also stably generate
\mathcal{C}
. We will now prove the required homological properties of the objects
\{\mathbb{S}_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq m}
, cf.
Definition

A.1
.(4), by an induction on the length
m
of the exceptional collection.

For the base case
m=1
, we have
\mathbb{S}_{1}\simeq\Delta_{1}
, which by exceptionality satisfies
\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{S}_{1},\mathbb{S}_{1})\simeq k
: the endomorphism object of
\mathbb{S}_{1}
is thus coconnective and so
\{\mathbb{S}_{1}\}
is a simple-minded collection.

For the induction step, from
m-1
to
m
, we proceed as follows. For
j<i
, consider the object

\mathbb{S}_{j,i}\coloneqq T_{\Delta_{j}}^{+,\geq 0}(\dots(T_{\Delta_{i-1}}^{+,\geq 0}(\Delta_{i})))\,.

By the induction assumption,
\mathbb{S}_{1},\dots,\mathbb{S}_{m-1}
forms a simple-minded collection in the
k
-linear subcategory of
\mathcal{C}
generated by
\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m-1}
. Applying the induction assumption to
\Delta_{2},\dots,\Delta_{m}
as well, we obtain that
\mathbb{S}_{2,2},\dots,\mathbb{S}_{2,m}
forms a simple-minded collection. Let
j\leq m-1
. Since
\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Delta_{i},\Delta_{1})\simeq 0
for all
i>1
, we have

\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{S}_{2,m}[-1],\mathbb{S}_{j})\simeq\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{S}_{2,m}[-1],\mathbb{S}_{2,j})\,.

The first term in the fiber and cofiber sequence

\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{S}_{2,m}[-1],\mathbb{S}_{j})\shortrightarrow\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{S}_{m}[-1],\mathbb{S}_{j})\shortrightarrow\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\tau_{\geq 0}(\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Delta_{1},\mathbb{S}_{2,m}))\otimes\Delta_{1},\mathbb{S}_{j})

(201)

is thus coconnective. The third term is also coconnective, as follows from the fact that
\tau_{\geq 0}(\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Delta_{1},\mathbb{S}_{2,m}))
is connective and the equivalences

\displaystyle\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Delta_{1},\mathbb{S}_{j})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{cof}(\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Delta_{1},\tau_{\geq 0}(\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Delta_{1},\mathbb{S}_{2,j}))\otimes\Delta_{1})\to\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Delta_{1},\mathbb{S}_{2,j}))

\displaystyle\simeq\tau_{<0}(\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Delta_{1},\mathbb{S}_{2,j}))\,.

The coconnectivity of the outer terms in (
201
) implies that the middle term
\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{S}_{m}[-1],\mathbb{S}_{j})
is coconnective. A similar computation shows that
\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{S}_{m},\mathbb{S}_{m})
is also coconnective.

It remains to prove that
\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{S}_{j}[-1],\mathbb{S}_{m})
is coconnective as well for
j\in[1,m-1]
. In the case of
j=1
, using that
\mathbb{S}_{1}\simeq\Delta_{1}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{S}_{1}[-1],\mathbb{S}_{m})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Delta_{1}[-1],\mathbb{S}_{m})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{cof}(\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Delta_{1}[-1],\tau_{\geq 0}(\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Delta_{1},\mathbb{S}_{2,m}))\otimes\Delta_{1})\to\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{1}[-1],\mathbb{S}_{2,m}))

\displaystyle\simeq\tau_{<0}(\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Delta_{1},\mathbb{S}_{2,m}))[1]

is indeed coconnective. Note that

\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{S}_{2,j}[-1],\mathbb{S}_{m})\simeq\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{S}_{2,j}[-1],\mathbb{S}_{2,m})

is coconnective by induction. For higher
j\in[1,m-1]
, the coconnectivity of
\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{S}_{j}[-1],\mathbb{S}_{m})
follows from the coconnectivity of the two other terms in the following fiber and cofiber sequence:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\tau_{\geq 0}(\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{S}_{2,j},\Delta_{1}))\otimes\Delta_{1}[-1],\mathbb{S}_{m})[-1]\to\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{S}_{j}[-1],\mathbb{S}_{m})\to\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{S}_{2,j}[-1],\mathbb{S}_{m})\,.

Finally, we must show that
\mathbb{S}_{i}
is indecomposable. By contradiction, let us suppose that
\mathbb{S}_{i}
is decomposable and let
j<i
be maximal such that
\mathbb{S}_{j,i}
is decomposable. Since
\mathbb{S}_{j+1,i}
is indecomposable by assumption, and there exists a recollement of the stable subcategory
\langle\Delta_{j},\dots,\Delta_{m}\rangle
into
\langle\Delta_{j}\rangle
and
\langle\Delta_{j+1},\dots,\Delta_{m}\rangle
, there must exist a direct sum decomposition

Z\oplus Z^{\prime}\simeq\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{j},\mathbb{S}_{j+1,i})

such that the composite morphism

Z^{\prime}\otimes\Delta_{j}\to\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{j},\mathbb{S}_{j+1,i})\otimes\Delta_{j}\to\mathbb{S}_{j+1,i}

vanishes, thus giving rise to a direct summand
Z^{\prime}\otimes\Delta_{j}[1]\subset\mathbb{S}_{j,i}
. However, this is not possible as for any non-zero morphism
k\subset\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{j},\mathbb{S}_{j+1,i})
, the arising morphism

k\otimes\Delta_{i}\to\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{j},\mathbb{S}_{j+1,i})\otimes\Delta_{j}\to\mathbb{S}_{j+1,i}

is also non-zero.
∎

The analog of
Proposition

A.12
for silting collections is as follows.

Proposition A.13

(Silting from exceptional)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a proper
k
-linear stable
\infty
-category.

(1)

Let
\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m}
be a full exceptional collection in
\mathcal{C}
. For any
i\in[1,m]
, consider the object

\mathbb{P}_{i}\coloneqq T_{\Delta_{m}}^{-,>0}(\cdots(T_{\Delta_{i+1}}^{-,>0}(\Delta_{i})))\in\mathcal{C}\,.

Then the objects
\{\mathbb{P}_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq m}
form a silting collection in
\mathcal{C}
.

(2)

Let
\nabla_{1},\dots,\nabla_{m}
be a full co-exceptional collection in
\mathcal{C}
. For any
i\in[1,m]
, consider the object

\reflectbox{$\mathbb{P}$}_{i}\coloneqq T_{\nabla_{m}}^{+,<0}(\cdots(T_{\nabla_{i+1}}^{+,<0}(\nabla_{i})))\in\mathcal{C}\,.

Then the objects
\{\reflectbox{$\mathbb{P}$}_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq m}
form a silting collection in
\mathcal{C}
.

Proof.

The proof follows a similar strategy as the proof of
Proposition

A.12
. As in there, we only prove Part (1), since Part (2) follows from the same argument applied to the opposite
\infty
-category. For part (1), the objects
\{\mathbb{P}_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq m}
stably generate the exceptional collection, hence they also stably generate
\mathcal{C}
. We will now show that
\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{P}_{i},\mathbb{P}_{j})
is connective by an induction on the length
m
of the exceptional collection.

For the base case
m=1
,
\mathbb{P}_{1}=\Delta_{1}
is exceptional and hence a silting object. For the induction step, let us proceed as follows. For
j>i
, we denote

\mathbb{P}_{j,i}\coloneqq T_{\Delta_{j}}^{-,>0}(\cdots(T_{\Delta_{i+1}}^{-,>0}(\Delta_{i})))

where
\mathbb{P}_{i,i}=\Delta_{i}
. The objects
\mathbb{P}_{2},\dots,\mathbb{P}_{m}
form a silting collection in the stable subcategory generated by them, as do the objects
\mathbb{P}_{m-1,1},\dots,\mathbb{P}_{m-1,m-1}
. We must thus show that
\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{1},\mathbb{P}_{j})
and
\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{j},\mathbb{P}_{1})
are connective for all
j\in[1,m]
.

First, we observe that

\displaystyle\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{1},\mathbb{P}_{m})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{1},\Delta_{m})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{cof}(\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\tau_{>0}(\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{m-1,1},\Delta_{m})^{*})\otimes\Delta_{m},\Delta_{m})\to\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{m-1,1},\Delta_{m}))

\displaystyle\simeq\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{P}_{m-1,1},\Delta_{m})

is connective. Similarly, for
1\leq j<m
, the object

\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{j},\mathbb{P}_{m-1,1})\simeq\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{m-1,j},\mathbb{P}_{m-1,1})

is connective. For
j=m
, we have
\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{m},\mathbb{P}_{m-1,1})\simeq 0
. Therefore, for any
j\in[1,m]
, the middle term in the exact sequence

\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{j},\tau_{>0}(\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{m-1,1},\Delta_{m})^{*})[-1]\otimes\Delta_{m})\to\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{j},\mathbb{P}_{1})\to\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{j},\mathbb{P}_{m-1,1})

must be connective, because the outer terms are as well. Thus
\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{j},\mathbb{P}_{1})
is connective for all
j\in[1,m]
. In order to show that
\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{1},\mathbb{P}_{j})
is connective, we proceed similarly. For all
j\in[2,m-1]
, we have the equivalence

\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{1},\mathbb{P}_{m-1,j})\simeq\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{m-1,1},\mathbb{P}_{m-1,j})\,.

In the fiber and cofiber sequence

\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{1},\tau_{>0}(\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{m-1,j},\Delta_{m})^{*})\otimes\Delta_{m})[-1]\to\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{1},\mathbb{P}_{j})\to\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{1},\mathbb{P}_{m-1,j})

the outer terms are thus connective, and hence so is the middle term
\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{1},\mathbb{P}_{j})
for any
j\in[1,m]
. This conclude the argument for connectivity. The indecomposablity of
\mathbb{P}_{i}
follows from a similar argument as the proof of the indecomposability of
\mathbb{S}_{i}
in
Proposition

A.12
.
∎

Remark A.14
.

Note that, in contrast to
Proposition

A.12
, cf.
Lemma

A.22
, the two silting collections in
Proposition

A.13
are typically different. This is a stable
\infty
-category analogue of the fact that the collections of projective and injective objects in the setting of abelian categories are typically different.∎

Let us now show in
Theorem

A.21
that the simple-minded collection in
Proposition

A.12
, and the silting collection in
Proposition

A.13
, each associated with the same full exceptional collection, are Koszul dual to each other. For that, we compare the associated
t
-structures. The argument for
Lemma

A.22
will similarly use
t
-structures.

Note that a
t
-structure on a stable
\infty
-category is by definition a
t
-structure on the triangulated homotopy category, see for instance
[
Lur17
, Def. 1.2.1.4]
. All
t
-structures will be homological, meaning that the positive aisle is stable under suspension. First, we recall how to glue
t
-structure via recollement:

Proposition A.15

(
[
BBD82
, Thm. 1.4.10]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a stable
\infty
-category with a recollement

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{!}}

into two stable subcategories
\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathcal{C}_{2}
with
i^{*}\dashv i_{*}\dashv i^{!}
and
j_{!}\dashv j^{*}\dashv j_{*}
. Suppose that
\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathcal{C}_{2}
are equipped with
t
-structures. Then there is a
t
-structure on
\mathcal{C}
with aisles

\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}=\{X\in\mathcal{C}|j^{*}(X)\in(\mathcal{C}_{2})_{\geq 0},i^{*}(X)\in(\mathcal{C}_{1})_{\geq 0}\}

and

\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}=\{X\in\mathcal{C}|j^{*}(X)\in(\mathcal{C}_{2})_{\leq 0},i^{!}(X)\in(\mathcal{C}_{1})_{\leq 0}\}\,.

∎

We apply
Proposition

A.15
to construct a
t
-structure
t(\Delta)
on
\mathcal{C}
from an exceptional collection
\Delta=(\Delta_{1},\ldots,\Delta_{m})
in
\mathcal{C}
, as follows.

Construction A.16
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a
k
-linear stable
\infty
-category with a full exceptional collection
\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m}
. For any
1\leq i\leq j\leq m
, we denote by
\langle\Delta_{i},\dots,\Delta_{j}\rangle
the
k
-linear stable subcategory of
\mathcal{C}
generated by
\Delta_{i},\dots,\Delta_{j}
. Consider the iterated recollements

where
i^{*}
and
j_{!}
are the fully faithful inclusions commuting with the inclusions into
\mathcal{C}
. By considering the standard
t
-structure on
\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(k)\simeq\langle\Delta_{i+1}\rangle
, and iteratively gluing
t
-structures via
Proposition

A.15
, we obtain a
t
-structure on
\mathcal{C}
from the full exceptional collection
\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m}
.∎

Remark A.17
.

The
t
-structure from
A.16
coincides with the
t
-structure obtained by iteratively gluing
t
-structures in the opposite direction, i.e. along the following recollements:

∎

Let us now prove that the objects
\mathbb{S}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}
forming the simple-minded collection associated to a full exceptional collection
\Delta=(\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m})
, cf.
Proposition

A.12
, are indeed in the heart
\mathcal{H}(\Delta)\subseteq\mathcal{C}
of the
t
-structure
t(\Delta)
from
A.16
.

Lemma A.18
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a proper
k
-linear stable
\infty
-category with a full exceptional collection
\Delta\coloneqq(\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m})
. Consider the associated heart
\mathcal{H}(\Delta)\subseteq\mathcal{C}
and the associated simple-minded collection
\mathbb{S}_{1},\ldots,\mathbb{S}_{m}
. Then:

1.

Each object
\mathbb{S}_{i}\in\mathcal{C}
lies in the heart
\mathcal{H}(\Delta)
.

2.

Each
\mathbb{S}_{i}\in\mathcal{H}(\Delta)
is a simple object.

Proof.

For Part (1), let us prove by a descending induction on
j\in[1,m]
that, for all
i\in[j,m]
, the object

\mathbb{S}_{j,i}\coloneqq T_{\Delta_{j}}^{+,\geq 0}(\cdots(T_{\Delta_{i-1}}^{+,\geq 0}(\Delta_{i}))

lies in the corresponding heart
\langle\Delta_{j},\dots,\Delta_{m}\rangle^{\heartsuit}\coloneqq\mathcal{H}(\Delta_{j},\dots,\Delta_{m})
. Indeed, for
j=i
, we have
\mathbb{S}_{i,i}=\Delta_{i}=i_{*}(\Delta_{i})
, and thus

i^{!}(\mathbb{S}_{i,i})\simeq i^{*}(\mathbb{S}_{i,i})\simeq\Delta_{i}\in\langle\Delta_{i}\rangle^{\heartsuit}

and
j^{*}(\mathbb{S}_{i,i})\simeq 0
. By using that the morphism
\mathbb{S}_{j+1,i}\to\mathbb{S}_{j,i}
describes the counit map of the adjunction
j_{!}\dashv j^{*}
evaluated at
\mathbb{S}_{j,i}
, we further obtain the following three inclusions:

\displaystyle j^{*}(\mathbb{S}_{j,i})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathbb{S}_{j+1,i}\in\langle\Delta_{j+1},\dots,\Delta_{m}\rangle^{\heartsuit},

\displaystyle i^{*}(\mathbb{S}_{j,i})

\displaystyle\simeq\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{j},\mathbb{S}_{j+1,i})\otimes\Delta_{j}[1]\in\langle\Delta_{j}\rangle_{\geq 0},

\displaystyle i^{!}(\mathbb{S}_{j,i})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{cof}(\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{j},\mathbb{S}_{j+1,i})\otimes\Delta_{j}\to\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{j},\mathbb{S}_{j+1,i})\otimes\Delta_{j})

\displaystyle\simeq\tau_{<0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{j},\mathbb{S}_{j+1,i})\otimes\Delta_{j}\in\langle\Delta_{j}\rangle_{\leq 0}\,.

By the inductive hypothesis on
\langle\Delta_{j+1},\dots,\Delta_{m}\rangle^{\heartsuit}
and the equivalences above, Part (1) thus follows.

For Part (2), we proceed by a descending induction on
j
to show that
\mathbb{S}_{j,i}\in\langle\Delta_{j},\dots,\Delta_{m}\rangle^{\heartsuit}
is simple. For that, given an epimorphism
\mathbb{S}_{j,i}\to X
, we must show that it is either zero or an isomorphism. Since
j^{*}
restricts to an exact functor between the hearts,
\mathbb{S}_{j+1,i}\to j^{*}(X)
is also an epimorphism. Note that
\mathbb{S}_{j+1,i}
is simple by the induction hypothesis and thus this later map must be either zero or an isomorphism. We treat these two cases:

Case 1:
Suppose that
j^{*}(X)\simeq\mathbb{S}_{j+1,i}
. Then the kernel
K
of
\mathbb{S}_{j,i}\to X
lies in the image of
i_{*}
. Thus
i^{*}(K)=i^{!}(K)\in\langle\Delta_{j}\rangle^{\heartsuit}
. Since the functor
i^{!}
is left exact when restricted to the hearts, we deduce that
i^{!}(K)\subset H_{0}(i^{!}(\mathbb{S}_{j,i}))\simeq 0
vanishes. Thus we obtain that the epimorphism
\mathbb{S}_{j,i}\longrightarrow X
is an isomorphism.

Case 2:
Suppose that
j^{*}(X)\simeq 0
. Since
i^{*}
is right exact when restricted to the hearts, there must exist an epimorphism from the degree
0
part of
i^{*}(\mathbb{S}_{j,i})\simeq\tau_{\geq 0}\operatorname{Mor}(\Delta_{j},\mathbb{S}_{j+1,i})\otimes\Delta_{j}[1]
to the degree
0
part of
i^{*}X
, and thus
\tau_{\leq 0}(i^{*}X)\simeq 0
. Since
i^{!}(X)\simeq i^{*}(X)\in\langle\Delta_{j}\rangle^{\heartsuit}
, we obtain
X=0
.

Therefore, assuming
\mathbb{S}_{j+1,i}
is simple, we have proven that any epimorphism
\mathbb{S}_{j,i}\to X
must be zero or an isomorphism, so that
\mathbb{S}_{j,i}
is simple as well. Hence, by induction,
\mathbb{S}_{j,i}
is simple for all
j\in[1,i]
. Since
\mathbb{S}_{i}\simeq\mathbb{S}_{1,i}
, this proves Part (2).
∎

A.16
provides a
t
-structure
t(\Delta)
from a full exceptional collection
\Delta\coloneqq(\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m})
. In line with this, a silting object also gives rise to a
t
-structure:

Lemma A.19

(
[
BY14
, Thm. A.1]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a
k
-linear stable
\infty
-category and
P\in\mathcal{C}
a silting object. Then there is a
t
-structure
t(P)
on
\mathcal{C}
with aisles

\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}=\{X\in\mathcal{C}|\operatorname{Ext}^{i}(P,X)\simeq 0\text{ for all }i\geq 1\}\,,

\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}=\{X\in\mathcal{C}|\operatorname{Ext}^{i}(P,X)\simeq 0\text{ for all }i\leq-1\}\,.

If the silting object
P
in
Lemma

A.19
is associated to a full exceptional collection
\Delta\coloneqq(\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m})
, via
Proposition

A.13
, the
t
-structure
t(P)
in
Lemma

A.19
coincides with the
t
-structure
t(\Delta)
from
A.16
, as we now prove.

Lemma A.20
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a proper
k
-linear stable
\infty
-category with a full exceptional collection
\Delta=(\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m})
, and consider the silting object
P(\Delta)\coloneqq\bigoplus_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{P}_{i}
associated to
\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m}
. Then the
t
-structure
t(P(\Delta))
coincides with the
t
-structure
t(\Delta)
.

Proof.

Let us denote
\mathbb{P}_{j,i}\coloneqq T^{-,>0}_{\Delta_{j}}(\cdots(T_{\Delta_{i+1}}^{-,>0}(\Delta_{i})))
for
i\leq j\in[1,m]
. Note that
\mathbb{P}_{j,1},\dots,\mathbb{P}_{j,j}\in\langle\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{j}\rangle
forms a silting collection for all
j\in[1,m]
. Let us prove by induction on
m
that the
t
-structrue induced by this silting collection coincides with the glued
t
-structure. For the base case
m=1
,
Y_{1,1}=\Delta_{1}
and thus the assertion holds.

For the induction step, suppose that the statement holds for
m-1
. That is, we assume that the two
t
-structures on
\langle\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m-1}\rangle
coincide. Now, for an object
X\in\mathcal{C}
, we have
X\in\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}
if
j^{*}(X)\in\langle\Delta_{m}\rangle_{\leq 0}
and
i^{!}(X)\in\langle\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m-1}\rangle_{\leq 0}
. Since
\Delta_{m}=\mathbb{P}_{m}
, the functor
j^{*}
identifies
\mathbb{P}_{m}
with
\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{m},\mathchar 45\relax)
under the equivalence
\langle\Delta_{m}\rangle\simeq\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(k)
. Now, for any
i\in[1,m-1]
, consider the fiber and cofiber sequence

\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{m-1,i},X)\to\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{i},X)\to\operatorname{Mor}(\tau_{>0}(\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{m-1,i},\Delta_{m})^{*})\otimes\Delta_{m},X)[1].

Suppose that
X
lies in the negative aisle of either of the two
t
-structures. Then the right term is coconnective, and the middle term is thus coconnective if and only if the left term is coconnective.
Since
\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{m-1,i},X)\in\mathcal{C}
lies in the image of
i_{*}
, we obtain the equivalence

\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{m-1,i},X)\simeq\operatorname{Mor}_{\langle\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m-1}\rangle}(\mathbb{P}_{m-1,i},i^{!}(X))\,.

Thus,
i^{!}(X)\in\langle\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m-1}\rangle_{\leq 0}
if and only if
\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{m-1,i},X)
is coconnective, showing that the negative isles of the two
t
-structures on
\langle\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m-1},\Delta_{m}\rangle
coincide, and hence
t(\Delta)=t(P(\Delta))
.
∎

Theorem A.21
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a proper
k
-linear stable
\infty
-category.

(1)

Let
\{\Delta_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq m}
be a full exceptional collection in
\mathcal{C}
,
\{\mathbb{S}_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq m}
its associated simple-minded collection, and
\{\mathbb{P}_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq m}
its associated silting collection. Then

\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{i},\mathbb{S}_{i})\simeq\begin{cases}k&i=j,\\
0&\text{else}\,,\end{cases}\qquad\forall i,j\in[1,m].

That is, the silting collection in
\mathcal{C}
associated to a full exceptional collection is Koszul dual to the simple-minded collection associated to that same exceptional collection.

(2)

Let
\{\nabla_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq m}
be a full co-exceptional collection in
\mathcal{C}
,
\{\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq m}
its associated simple-minded collection, and
\{\reflectbox{$\mathbb{P}$}_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq m}
its associated silting collection. Then

\operatorname{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{i},\reflectbox{$\mathbb{P}$}_{j})\simeq\begin{cases}k&i=j,\\
0&\text{else}\,,\end{cases}\qquad\forall i,j\in[1,m].

That is, the silting collection in
\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{op}}
associated to a full co-exceptional collection is Koszul dual to the simple-minded collection associated to that same co-exceptional collection.

Proof.

Via the bijection from
Theorem

A.9
, silting collections with
m
indecomposable objects correspond to simple-minded collections with
m
indecomposable objects, with the latter given by the simple objects in the heart arising from the former. Part (1) thus follows from combining
Lemma

A.18
and
Lemma

A.20
. Part (2) follows from the same argument applied to
\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{op}}
.
∎

Let us finally conclude that the two simple-minded collections from
Proposition

A.12
, built either from a full exceptional collection or its dual co-exceptional collection, coincide.

Lemma A.22
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a
k
-linear stable
\infty
-category with a full exceptional collection
\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m}
and dual full co-exceptional collection
\nabla_{1},\dots,\nabla_{m}
. Then the two simple-minded collections from
Proposition

A.12
coincide, i.e.
\mathbb{S}_{i}\simeq\reflectbox{$\mathbb{S}$}_{i}
for all
i\in[1,m]
.

Proof.

By
Lemma

A.18
, it suffices to show that the
t
-structure
t(\Delta)
arising from
\Delta=(\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m})
of
\mathcal{C}
agrees with the
t
-structure
t(\nabla)
on
\mathcal{C}
arising from
\nabla=(\nabla_{1},\dots,\nabla_{m})\in\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{op}}
after passing to the opposite
\infty
-category.

Let us prove by this by induction on
m
. For the base case
m=1
,
\Delta_{1}=\nabla_{1}
, and thus the assertion holds. For the induction step, from
m-1
to
m
, we proceed as follows, The
t
-structure
t(\nabla)
on
\mathcal{C}
arises after passing to opposite categories from gluing
t
-structures along the recollement

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\nabla_{1},\dots,\nabla_{m-1}\rangle^{\operatorname{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\langle\nabla_{1},\dots,\nabla_{m}\rangle^{\operatorname{op}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\langle\nabla_{m}\rangle^{\operatorname{op}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{i}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{i}^{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{i}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{j}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{j}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{j}_{!}}

where
\hat{i}_{*}
and
\hat{j}_{!}
are the fully faithful inclusions commuting with the given inclusions into
\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{op}}
.

Now, the functor
(\hat{i}_{*})^{\operatorname{op}}
identifies with the functor
i_{*}\colon\langle\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m-1}\rangle\subset\mathcal{C}
, both describing the canonical inclusions of
\langle\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m-1}\rangle=\langle\nabla_{1},\dots,\nabla_{m-1}\rangle
, which coincide by the inductive hypothesis. Since passing to opposite categories exchanges left and right adjoints, we thus also have
(\hat{i}^{!})^{\operatorname{op}}\simeq i^{*}
.
Furthermore, under the equivalence
\langle\nabla_{m}\rangle\simeq\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(k)\simeq\langle\Delta_{m}\rangle
, the functor
(\hat{j}^{*})^{\operatorname{op}}
identifies with
j^{*}
, since it is given by the Verdier quotient functor by
i_{*}
.

Since passing to opposite categories exchanges the left and right aisles, an object
X\in\langle\nabla_{1},\dots,\nabla_{m}\rangle
lies in the positive aisle if and only if

i^{!}(X)\simeq(\hat{i}^{!})^{\operatorname{op}}(X)\in(\langle\nabla_{1},\dots,\nabla_{m-1}\rangle^{\operatorname{op}}_{\leq 0})^{\operatorname{op}}=\langle\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m-1}\rangle_{\geq 0}

and

j^{*}(X)\simeq(\hat{j}^{*})^{\operatorname{op}}(X)\in(\langle\nabla_{m}\rangle^{\operatorname{op}}_{\leq 0})^{\operatorname{op}}=\langle\Delta_{m}\rangle_{\geq 0}\,.

Thus, the two
t
-structures coincide.
∎

A.3
Relation with highest weight theory

Highest weight categories were introduced in
[
CPS88
]
. The following definition uses a slightly different formulation given in
[
Kra17
]
, which itself refers to
[
Rou08
]
. We will further only consider highest weight categories whose poset is a totally ordered set on
m
elements. The full highest weight theory for more general, possibly infinite, posets can be found in
[
BS24
]
.

Definition A.23

(Highest weight structure)
.

Let
A
be a
k
-linear abelian category having only finitely many isoclasses of simple objects and finite dimensional Homs. A highest weight structure on
A
consists of a collection of objects
\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m}\in A
, satisfying the following conditions:

•

\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(\Delta_{i},\Delta_{i})\simeq k
for all
1\leq i\leq m
.

•

\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(\Delta_{i},\Delta_{j})=0
for all
1\leq j<i\leq m
.

•

For all
1\leq i\leq m
, there exists a projective cover
P_{i}\to\Delta_{i}
whose kernel has a filtration whose associated graded consists of objects
\{\Delta_{j}\}_{i<j\leq m}
.

•

The object
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{m}P_{i}
is a projective generator of
A
, meaning that an object
X\in A
vanishes if and only if
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{m}P_{i},X)=0
.∎

If an abelian category
A
has a highest weight structure, then the images of the objects
\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m}
under the inclusion
N(A)\subset\mathcal{D}^{b}(A)
, with
N(A)
the nerve of
A
, form a full exceptional collection, see for instance
[
Kra17
, Thm. 5.2]
. These objects are called the standard objects in
A
. Furthermore, we can label the iso-classes of simple objects in
A
by
S_{1},\dots,S_{m}
, and for all
i\in[1,m]
there exists an epimorphism
\Delta_{i}\to S_{i}
in
A
whose kernel has composition factors in
\{S_{i}\}_{1\leq j<i}
.

Remark A.24
.

A highest weight structure, as in
Definition

A.23
, can equivalently be described as a quasi-hereditary structure on the endomorphism algebra of the projective generator, cf.
[
CPS88
]
.∎

The results of
Section

A.2
in the stable setting are inspired by the notion of a highest weight abelian category. The full exceptional collection acquires the role of the standard objects, the associated simple-minded collection has the role of the simple objects, and the associated silting collection has the role of the projective objects. In the following results, we elucidate the precise relationship between the constructions from
Section

A.2
and the theory of highest weight categories.

Lemma A.25
.

Let
A
be a
k
-linear abelian category equipped with a highest weight structure with standard objects
\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m}\in A
. Then the silting collection
\{\mathbb{P}_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq m}
in
\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{b}}(A)
from
Proposition

A.13
associated with the full exceptional collection
\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m}\in\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{b}}(A)
describes the image of the collection of projective
A
-modules
\{P_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq m}
under the inclusion
N(A)\subset\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{b}}(A)
.

Proof.

Let us denote
\mathbb{P}_{j,i}\coloneqq T^{-,>0}_{\Delta_{j}}(\cdots(T_{\Delta_{i+1}}^{-,>0}(\Delta_{i})))
for
1\leq i\leq j\leq m
. For
1\leq j\leq m
, let
A_{\leq j}\subset A
be the abelian subcategory generated by
\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{j}
. Let us prove by induction on
j
that
\{\mathbb{P}_{j,i}\}_{1\leq i\leq j}
describes the images of the projectives in
A_{\leq j}
in
\mathcal{D}^{b}(A_{\leq j})\subset\mathcal{D}^{b}(A)
. The base case
j=1
readily holds.

For the inductive step, we proceed as follows. First, the projective objects in a highest weight abelian category arise via iterated maximal extensions between the standard objects. Indeed, this follows for instance from the construction of the tilting modules described in the proof of
[
Soe99
, Proposition 3.1]
applied to the Ringel dual highest weight category. Alternatively, a direct construction can also be found in
[
Kra22
, Ex. 8.1.10]
, where these maximal extensions are called universal extensions. Now, since
A_{\leq j+1}
is highest weight, the projective objects in
A_{\leq j+1}
arise from the projective objects in
A_{\leq j}
by universal extension with
\operatorname{add}(\Delta_{j+1})
, as well as the object
\Delta_{j+1}
itself. Since
\mathbb{P}_{j,i}
is projective in
A_{\leq j}
, the Statement 1 in
[
DR89
, Appendix]
implies that

\operatorname{Ext}^{l}(\mathbb{P}_{j,i},\Delta_{j+1})\simeq 0,\qquad l\in[2,m]\,.

Thus,
\tau_{>0}\operatorname{Mor}(\mathbb{P}_{j,i},\Delta_{j+1})^{*}
is concentrated in degree
1
. This shows that
\mathbb{P}_{j+1,i}=T^{-,>0}_{\Delta_{j+1}}(\mathbb{P}_{j,i})
corresponds to the passage to the universal extension, thus describing a projective object in
A_{\leq j+1}
.
∎

Lemma A.26
.

Let
A
be a
k
-linear abelian category equipped with a highest weight structure, and standard objects
\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m}\in A
. Then the simple-minded collection
\{\mathbb{S}_{j}\}_{1\leq j\leq m}
in
\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{b}}(A)
from
Proposition

A.12
associated with the full exceptional collection
\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m}
coincides with the image of the collection of simple modules
\{S_{j}\}_{1\leq j\leq m}
in
A
under the inclusion
N(A)\subset\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{b}}(A)
.

Proof.

This follows from combining
Theorem

A.9
,
Theorem

A.21
and
Lemma

A.25
.
∎

Finally, we give a characterization of highest weight structures on hearts arising from full exceptional collections as follows. Given a proper
k
-linear stable
\infty
-category with a full exceptional collection
\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m}
, we denote as above

\mathbb{P}_{j,i}\coloneqq T^{-,>0}_{\Delta_{j}}(\cdots(T_{\Delta_{i+1}}^{-,>0}(\Delta_{i}))),\qquad 1\leq i\leq j\leq m.

Note that
\mathbb{P}_{j,1},\dots,\mathbb{P}_{j,j}
defines a silting collection in
\langle\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{j}\rangle
for all
j\in[1,m]
. Recall that we denote by
t(\Delta)
the
t
-structure associated to a full exceptional collection
\Delta=(\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m})
via
A.16
.

Theorem A.27
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a proper
k
-linear stable
\infty
-category with a full exceptional collection
\Delta=(\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m})
. The following are equivalent:

(1)

For all
j\in[1,m]
, the homology of the derived endomorphism algebra of the silting object
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{j}\mathbb{P}_{j,i}
in
\langle\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{j}\rangle
is concentrated in degree
0
.

(2)

For all
j\in[1,m]
, the silting object
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{j}\mathbb{P}_{j,i}
in
\langle\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{j}\rangle
lies in the heart of the
t
-structure
t(\Delta_{1},\ldots,\Delta_{j})
.

(3)

There exists a
k
-linear abelian category
A
with a highest weight structure with standard objects
\Delta_{1}^{A},\dots,\Delta_{m}^{A}
and a fully faithful functor
N(A)\subset\mathcal{C}
, which induces an equivalence of stable
\infty
-categories
F\colon\mathcal{D}^{b}(A)\to\mathcal{C}
satisfying that
F(\Delta_{i}^{A})\simeq\Delta_{i}
.

Proof.

If the derived endomorphism algebra
R
of a silting object is concentrated in degree
0
, then the heart of the standard
t
-structure on
\mathcal{D}^{\operatorname{perf}}(R)
contains
R
, as
R
is then a dg module over itself in degree
0
. This proves (1)
\Rightarrow
(2).

For (2)
\Rightarrow
(3) we proceed as follows. Let
A
be the heart of the
t
-structure from Lemma
A.19
and
A_{\leq j}
be the heart of
\langle\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{j}\rangle
,
j\in[1,m]
. Note that
A_{\leq j}\subset A
. To establish (3), assuming (2), we verify the conditions from Definition
A.23
. First, the silting object
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{P}_{i}
is a projective generator of
A
, which establishes the fourth condition in Definition
A.23
. Since
\Delta_{j}=\mathbb{P}_{j,j}\in A_{\leq j}
, we obtain
\Delta_{j}\in A
. Further, since these objects form a full exceptional collection in
\mathcal{C}
, we conclude that

\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(\Delta_{i},\Delta_{j})\simeq\begin{cases}k&i=j\\
0&j<i\end{cases}\,.

This establishes the first and second conditions in Definition
A.23
. To verify the third condition, it remains to show that the morphism
\mathbb{P}_{i}\to\Delta_{i}
is an epimorphism in
A
whose kernel has a filtration whose associated graded consists of standard objects. Since
\mathbb{P}_{j+1,i}\in A_{\leq j+1}
, we deduce that
j^{*}(\mathbb{P}_{j+1,i})\in\langle\Delta_{j+1}\rangle^{\heartsuit}.
Thus, for all
1\leq i\leq j<m
, we have a fiber and cofiber sequence in
\mathcal{C}

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{j,i},\Delta_{j+1})^{*}\otimes\Delta_{j+1}\to\mathbb{P}_{j+1,i}\to\mathbb{P}_{j,i}

where
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbb{P}_{j,i},\Delta_{j+1})^{*}\otimes\Delta_{j+1}\simeq j_{!}j^{*}(\mathbb{P}_{j+1,i})
. Since the fiber and cofiber sequence lies in the image of
N(A)\subset\mathcal{D}^{b}(A)
, it arises from an exact sequence in
A
. This shows that
\mathbb{P}_{j+1,i}\to\mathbb{P}_{j,i}
is an epimorphism. The morphism
\mathbb{P}_{i}\to\Delta_{i}
is thus an epimorphism, as it is the composite of epimorphisms, and the kernel has the desired filtration. This proves (2)
\Rightarrow
(3).

Finally, for (3)
\Rightarrow
(1), we suppose that (3) is satisfied. Then
A_{\leq j}\subset A
inherits a highest weight structure with standard objects
\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{j}
. By
Lemma

A.25
, the silting object in
\mathcal{D}^{b}(A_{\leq j})
then coincides with the image of the projective objects in
A_{\leq j}
in
\mathcal{D}^{b}(A_{\leq j})
. Thus, the derived endomorphism algebra is concentrated in degree
0
, showing that (1) holds.
∎

Remark A.28
.

Let
A^{\prime}
be an abelian category and
\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m}\in A^{\prime}
a collection of objects giving rise to a full exceptional collection in
\mathcal{D}^{b}(A^{\prime})
. Then
[
Kra17
, Theorem 5.2]
gives a further homological criterion for when these objects describe the standard objects in a highest weight structure on the module category of the associated silting object: this is the case if and only if the exceptional collection is strictly full, which coarsely means that the higher extension groups in the exact category of objects filtered by
\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{m}
agree with those in
\mathcal{D}^{b}(A^{\prime})
.

Appendix B
Coherent chain complexes and grafting

The goal of this appendix is to introduce bounded coherent chain complexes in stable
\infty
-categories, define their totalization, and describe their grafting. In the main text, we will repeatedly use grafting of coherent chain complexes, as described in
Lemma

B.8
. Let us denote
I\coloneqq N(\{1\to 0\})\simeq(\Delta^{1})^{\operatorname{op}}
.

Definition B.1
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a stable
\infty
-category and
n\geq 0
.

(1)

An
n
-cube in
\mathcal{D}
is a functor
I^{n}\to\mathcal{D}
. We denote by
\operatorname{Cube}_{n}(\mathcal{D})\coloneqq\operatorname{Fun}(I^{n},\mathcal{D})
the
\infty
-category of
n
-cubes in
\mathcal{D}
.

(2)

A coherent chain complex
C_{\ast}
in
\mathcal{D}
concentrated in degrees
0
through
n
consists of an
n
-cube
C_{\ast}\colon I^{n}\to\mathcal{D}
, satisfying that
C_{\bf J}
is zero for all

{\bf J}\in\{0,1\}^{n}\backslash\bigcup_{0\leq i\leq n}\{(0,\dots,\underbrace{0}_{\operatorname{pos.\penalty\ i}},1,\dots,1)\}\,.

We denote by

\operatorname{Ch}_{\geq 0,\leq n}(\mathcal{D})\subset\operatorname{Cube}_{n}(\mathcal{D})

the full subcategory spanned by coherent chain complexes concentrated in degrees
0
through
n
. ∎

Remark B.2
.

Definition

B.1
differs a priori from the standard definition of a coherent chain complex given for instance in
[
Wal22
, Def. 2.3.5]
. Let us briefly explain why the two definitions are equivalent, as follows. Let
[0,n]^{\operatorname{op}}_{\ast}
denote the nerve of
1
-category obtained from the poset
[0,n]^{\operatorname{op}}
(with morphisms
i\to j
if
i>j
) by adjoining a zero object
\ast
and consider the quotient
{[0,n]^{\operatorname{op}}_{\ast}}_{/\sim}
obtained by identifying each composite
d^{2}\colon i\to i-2
with the zero morphism. The standard definition of coherent
(n+1)
-term chain complex in a stable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
is a pointed functor
{[0,n]^{\operatorname{op}}_{\ast}}_{/\sim}\to\mathcal{C}
, mapping
\ast
to
0
. We denote by
\operatorname{Fun}^{\circ}({[0,n]^{\operatorname{op}}_{\ast}}_{/\sim},\mathcal{C})
the
\infty
-category of such pointed functors.

Now, there is a functor
\pi\colon I^{n}\to{[0,n]^{\operatorname{op}}_{\ast}}_{/\sim}
, mapping each
{\bf J}\in\{0,1\}^{n}\backslash\bigcup_{0\leq i\leq n}\{(0,\dots,\underbrace{0}_{\operatorname{pos.\penalty\ i}},1,\dots,1)\}
to
\ast
and
(0,\dots,\underbrace{0}_{\operatorname{pos.\penalty\ i}},1,\dots,1)
to
n+1-i
. It can be shown that the functor
\pi
is the localization of
\infty
-categories at all morphisms mapped under
\pi
to
\operatorname{id}_{\ast}
, for instance by using
[
Lur24
,
Proposition 05ZD
]
and the observation that the full subcategory of
I^{n}
generated by
\pi^{-1}(\ast)
is weakly contractible. This thus yields the required equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\operatorname{Ch}_{\geq 0,\leq n}(\mathcal{D})\simeq\operatorname{Fun}^{\circ}({[0,n]^{\operatorname{op}}_{\ast}}_{/\sim},\mathcal{C})\,,

and both definitions of coherent chain complexes are equivalent.∎

Remark B.3
.

Combining the two versions of the
\infty
-categorical Dold–Kan correspondence,
[
Lur17
, Thm. 1.2.3.7]
and
[
Wal22
, Cor. 4.1.2]
, we find that for any stable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{D}
there exists an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\operatorname{Ch}_{\geq 0,\leq n}(\mathcal{D})\simeq\operatorname{Fun}([0,n],\mathcal{D})

between
(n+1)
-term coherent chain complexes and
(n+1)
-term filtered chain complexes. ∎

Remark B.4
.

Let
\operatorname{Fun}^{\circ}(I^{n+1}\backslash\{1,\dots,1\},\mathcal{D})\subset\operatorname{Fun}(I^{n+1}\backslash\{1,\dots,1\},\mathcal{D})
be the full subcategory spanned by morphisms
C_{\ast}\colon I^{n+1}\backslash\{1,\dots,1\}\to\mathcal{D}
satisfying that
C_{\bf J}\simeq 0
for all

{\bf J}\in I^{n+1}\backslash\bigcup_{0\leq i\leq n+1}\{(0,\dots,\underbrace{0}_{\operatorname{pos.\penalty\ i}},1,\dots,1)\}\,.

Each object in
\operatorname{Fun}^{\circ}(I^{n+1}\backslash\{1,\dots,1\},\mathcal{D})
is the right Kan extension of its restriction to
\{0\}\times I^{n}
, and the restriction functor

\operatorname{Fun}^{\circ}(I^{n+1}\backslash\{1,\dots,1\},\mathcal{D})\to\operatorname{Ch}_{\geq 0,\leq n}(\mathcal{D})

is thus an equivalence of
\infty
-categories, see
[
Lur24
,
Tag 030M
]
.∎

Let us now introduce the total cofiber of a coherent chain complex, as follows.

Definition B.5
.

We define the total fiber functor as the composite

\operatorname{tot-fib}\colon\operatorname{Ch}_{\geq 0,\leq n}(\mathcal{D})\simeq\operatorname{Fun}^{\circ}(I^{n+1}\backslash\{1,\dots,1\},\mathcal{D})\xlongrightarrow{\operatorname{lim}}\mathcal{D}\,.

The total cofiber functor is similarly defined as the composite

\operatorname{tot-cof}\colon\operatorname{Ch}_{\geq 0,\leq n}(\mathcal{D})\simeq\operatorname{Fun}^{\circ}(I^{n+1}\backslash\{0,\dots,0\},\mathcal{D})\xlongrightarrow{\operatorname{colim}}\mathcal{D}\,.

The total cofiber will be taken as the default totalization, and thus also denoted by
\operatorname{tot}\coloneqq\operatorname{tot-cof}
.∎

Remark B.6
.

The total (co)fiber of a coherent chain complex is equivalent to the iterated (co)fibers in the
n
coordinate directions of the corresponding cube, taken in any order, see e.g.
[
DJW19
, Cor. A.13]
. In particular, there is an equivalence
\operatorname{tot-cof}\simeq\operatorname{tot-fib}[n]
.
∎

Lemma B.7
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a stable
\infty
-category.

(1)

There exists a pullback diagram of stable
\infty
-categories:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Ch}_{\geq 0,\leq n+1}(\mathcal{D})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Fun}(I,\mathcal{D})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Ch}_{\geq 0,\leq n}(\mathcal{D})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{ev}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tot-fib}}

(2)

There exists a pullback diagram of stable
\infty
-categories:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Ch}_{\geq 0,\leq n+1}(\mathcal{D})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Ch}_{\geq 0,\leq n}(\mathcal{D})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Fun}(I,\mathcal{D})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tot-cof}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{ev}_{0}}

(3)

More generally, the limit of the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Ch}_{\geq 0,\leq n-i}(\mathcal{D})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Fun}(I,\mathcal{D})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Ch}_{\geq 0,\leq i}(\mathcal{D})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tot-cof}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{ev}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{ev}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tot-fib}}

is equivalent to
\operatorname{Ch}_{\geq 0,\leq n+1}
.

Proof.

For Part (1), since
I^{n+1}\simeq(I^{n+1}\backslash\{1,\dots,1\})^{\triangleleft}
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ch}_{\geq 0,\leq n+1}(\mathcal{D})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Fun}(I^{n+1}\backslash\{1,\dots,1\})^{\triangleleft},\mathcal{D})\times_{\operatorname{Fun}(I^{n+1}\backslash\{1,\dots,1\},\mathcal{D})}\operatorname{Fun}^{\circ}(I^{n+1}\backslash\{1,\dots,1\},\mathcal{D})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Fun}(I,\mathcal{D})\times_{\mathcal{D}}\operatorname{Fun}(I^{n+1}\backslash\{1,\dots,1\},\mathcal{D})\times_{\operatorname{Fun}(I^{n+1}\backslash\{1,\dots,1\},\mathcal{D})}\operatorname{Fun}^{\circ}(I^{n+1}\backslash\{1,\dots,1\},\mathcal{D})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Fun}(I,\mathcal{D})\times_{\mathcal{D}}\operatorname{Ch}_{\geq 0,\leq n}(\mathcal{D})\,,

where the second equivalence uses the universal property of the limit over
I^{n+1}\backslash\{1,\dots,1\}
. This establishes Part (1). The proof of Part (2) is similar. Part (3) can be reduced to an iterated application of Parts (1) and (2).
∎

Lemma

B.7
implies that we can “graft” coherent chain complexes as follows:

Lemma B.8
.

Let
m,n\geq 0
and
i\in[0,n]
. Let
D_{\ast}\in\operatorname{Ch}(\mathcal{C})_{\geq 0,\leq m}
and
C_{\ast}\in\operatorname{Ch}(\mathcal{C})_{\geq 0,\leq n}
, such that
C_{i}\simeq\operatorname{tot}(D_{\ast})
. Then there exists a canonical chain complex
\tilde{C}_{\ast}\in\operatorname{Ch}(\mathcal{C})_{\geq 0,\leq n+m}
with

\tilde{C}_{j}=\begin{cases}C_{j}&0\leq j<i\\
D_{j-i}&i\leq j\leq i+m\\
C_{j-m}[-m]&j>i+m\end{cases}

satisfying

\operatorname{tot}(\tilde{C}_{\ast})\simeq\operatorname{tot}(C_{\ast})
. The complex
\tilde{C}_{*}
is said to be the grafting of
D_{\ast}
onto
C_{\ast}
.

Proof.

There is a canonical map
\alpha\colon C_{i}\to\operatorname{tot-fib}(C_{\ast\leq i-1})
, which can be considered as an element
\alpha\in\operatorname{Fun}(I,\mathcal{D})
that satisfies
\operatorname{ev}_{0}(\alpha)\simeq\operatorname{tot-cof}(D_{\ast})
. From the limit diagram in
Lemma

B.7
.(3) we thus obtain a coherent chain complex
\tilde{C}_{\ast\leq i+m}\in\operatorname{Ch}_{\geq 0,\leq i+m}(\mathcal{D})
satisfying that

\operatorname{cof-tot}(\tilde{C}_{\ast\leq i+m})\simeq\operatorname{tot-cof}(C_{\ast\leq i})\,.

By
Lemma

B.7
.(3), there is a canonical morphism
\beta\colon\operatorname{tot-cof}(C_{\ast>i})\to\operatorname{fib-tot}(C_{\ast\leq i})
. Using the apparent equivalence
\operatorname{tot-fib}(\tilde{C}_{\ast\leq i+m})[m]\simeq\operatorname{tot-fib}(C_{\ast\leq i})
, the morphism
\beta
gives rise to a morphism
\gamma:\operatorname{tot-cof}(C_{\ast>j})[-m]\to\operatorname{tot-fib}(\tilde{C}_{\ast\leq i+m})
. By using
Lemma

B.7
.(3) a last time, we use the morphism
\gamma
to construct to the required coherent chain complex
\tilde{C}_{\ast}
with
\operatorname{tot}(\tilde{C}_{\ast})\simeq\operatorname{tot}(C_{\ast})
.∎
